YAWGMOTH — THE SCARAB SWARM

Yawgmoth, Thran Physician · B · 100 cards · March 2027

✓ RULES-VERIFIED◆ DATA-DERIVED★ COACHING

The PlanThe 99Keep or ShipThe LinesAt the TablePlayThe NumbersThe Record

The Plan

What this deck is trying to do, and what it is assuming away. ◆ DATA-DERIVED★ COACHING

The table will decide you are the Yawgmoth deck and point removal at your commander. Half the time, let them. He recasts from the command zone, and his free ability draws through a tax, through a lock, on any end step. He lands turn four in 7,188 of 10,000 goldfished games.

Half your kills need him standing: the Nest engine, the Mikaeus line, the undying pair. The two Gravecrawler loops and the Exquisite Blood pair never name him. Three cards protect him: Lightning Greaves, Supernatural Stamina, Malakir Rebirth — never spend one for value.

Six verified shapes, each ending the table at once — never aim at a seat. Two are unbounded: stack 009 funds itself with Treasure, stack 011 pays nothing and gains a life a lap. Stack 012 is lethal because it cannot stop. Two are library-bounded: stack 010 needs 39 activations of 59, stack 014 about 30 of 55. The sixth is bread and butter: Nest of Scarabs plus any drain, stacked across turns.

By turn 10 you have drawn a drain 82.3% of the time, renewable fodder 82.3%, a free outlet 78.5%, ignition 73.8%. The named kills read tiny beside that: 13.5% for the Nest engine unassisted, single digits for the rest. Read them one at a time; never add them up.

Your turns go to hunting the missing card: that is what five tutors are for, and one carries the Exquisite Blood kill from 6.6% to 42.6%. Never cast one for value either.

A wrath is not what beats you: Bastion of Remembrance, Zulaport Cutthroat, Blood Artist and The Meathook Massacre get paid when your creatures die, and Gravecrawler and Reassembling Skeleton rebuild cheap. A permanent you cannot remove does — the 99 answers one, and only an enchantment.

Pick the kill that dodges it: Exquisite Blood needs no graveyard, outlet or recursion. Deploy cheapest-to-answer first, drain last, and open Rule 0 at bracket 4: four Game Changers and eleven two-card infinite records force it.

What this does not model

  • Unsettled Does Ashnod's Altar + Pitiless Plunderer + Reassembling Skeleton hold as an unbounded line on a real board? Commander Spellbook rates it bracket 1 for infinite colorless mana and infinite death triggers, all three cards are in the 99, and the arithmetic looks self-funding (sacrifice for {C}{C} plus a Treasure, recur for {1}{B}), but nothing has resolved it and neither Plunderer nor the Skeleton appears in any of the eleven passing stacks.
  • Unsettled Does Mikaeus, the Unhallowed plus ONE free sacrifice outlet loop without the commander? Seven such pairings are in candidate_lines (Carrion Feeder, Viscera Seer, Woe Strider, Yahenni, Ashnod's Altar, Phyrexian Altar, Blasting Station) and all seven outlets are in the 99, but undying's intervening 'if' fails the moment the body carries a +1/+1 counter — which is exactly what stack 010 needed Yawgmoth's -1/-1 counter to strip.
  • Unsettled How many Nest of Scarabs rotations, across how many turns, does an average board actually need to kill a four-opponent pod? Stacks 002/003/004/005 each measure exactly ONE rotation from a three-body board — 5 to 8 activations, 7 to 13 drains, 28 to 52 life removed from 160 — and none of them carries the board into a second turn.
  • Simulation Simulates resource development, not full games (no interaction, no removal).

1 further question about this engine is open and unsettled.

Keep or Ship

What a keepable hand looks like, and how often you get one. ◆ DATA-DERIVED★ COACHING

Most people mulligan this deck hunting for the commander. Don't. He lands on turn four in 7,188 of 10,000 goldfished games with no help at all, so keep the hand that gives him something to eat, or the hand that wins without him.

Keep a shape, not a card list: something that sacrifices, something to sacrifice, and two to four lands. That is what 9,132 of 10,000 kept hands look like, and from there your turn-3 land drop hits 91.6%. The outlet and the fodder are interchangeable, so stop holding out for the good one. Carrion Feeder, Viscera Seer, Woe Strider, Yahenni and Blasting Station all do the outlet job; Bitterblossom, Reassembling Skeleton, Stitcher's Supplier, Ophiomancer and Gravecrawler are every one of them fodder. A free outlet turns up by turn 10 in 78.5% of games — you do not need the specific one in your opener.

Ship the hand that is all payoff, however good it looks. Exquisite Blood, Grave Titan and five lands is a mulligan: mean available mana is 3.908 on turn 4, so a five-drop with nothing under it means you do nothing for half the game. Enablers dig toward payoffs. Payoffs sit there waiting for an engine nobody built.

One land is a ship unless Sol Ring, Ancient Tomb or Dark Ritual is sitting next to it. Zero lands is always a ship, and only 284 opening sevens in 10,000 come out that way, so it will hardly ever be the decision in front of you. Ship freely in general — you keep the first seven 81.6% of the time at a mean of 0.222 mulligans, which makes the first one a trade you are allowed to take on instinct. And take the hand with a tutor over the hand with the commander every time: a tutor is what turns a 6.6% kill into a 42.6% one.

Keeps the first seven82%

The Lines

How it actually wins, resolved and checked against the rules. ✓ RULES-VERIFIED

Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Bastion of Remembrance

what bounds the engine?

The clause is “up to one target creature”, and targets are locked in when the ability is activated (CR 115.1c) — the Insect the Nest is about to make does not yet exist to be chosen. Protection from Humans keeps Yawgmoth off his own list (CR 702.16b). So the moment the opponent's creature is dead, every legal target is one of your own tokens: two bodies leave, one comes back. The holding: this engine draws cards and then stops. It is not a kill, and no ordering makes it one.

Fix the reading of the board before resolving anything, then account for what already happened during the activation itself. THE READING: the scenario's board line for Insect token A is annotated 'already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack', which matches stack[0] ('1 life paid (you are at 39) and Insect token A sacrificed') and the stated life total of 39. Insect A is therefore in the graveyard, not on the battlefield, and the creatures available to pay future sacrifice costs are Insect token B and the Human Soldier token - a THREE-body board counting the one already spent (A, B, Soldier), not four. Every figure below follows from that reading. Within the activation itself the order was: Yawgmoth's ability was put on the stack, its target (Grizzly Bears) was chosen, and only then were its costs paid - 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and the sacrifice of Insect token A.

Resolve
Step 1 of 11
Fix the reading of the board before resolving anything, then account for what already happened during the activation itself. THE READING: the scenario's board line for Insect token A is annotated 'already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack', which matches stack[0] ('1 life paid (you are at 39) and Insect token A sacrificed') and the stated life total of 39. Insect A is therefore in the graveyard, not on the battlefield, and the creatures available to pay future sacrifice costs are Insect token B and the Human Soldier token - a THREE-body board counting the one already spent (A, B, Soldier), not four. Every figure below follows from that reading. Within the activation itself the order was: Yawgmoth's ability was put on the stack, its target (Grizzly Bears) was chosen, and only then were its costs paid - 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and the sacrifice of Insect token A.
Insect token A was a creature you controlled that died, so Bastion of Remembrance's ability ('Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life') has triggered. This is a leaves-the-battlefield trigger and it looks back in time: the game consults the game state immediately before the sacrifice to see that the dying Insect was a creature YOU controlled, which is what licenses this trigger and the six that follow it - SEVEN Bastion triggers in all across the five completed activations. It triggered during the process of activating an ability, so it did nothing at that moment; it waits and is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority, which is immediately after the activation is complete. It goes on top of Yawgmoth's ability. That the sacrificed creature was a TOKEN does not suppress this: a token that changes zones triggers applicable triggered abilities before it ceases to exist - the point that carries every one of the seven drains in this line, since all seven creatures of yours that die here are tokens. Stack, bottom to top: [0] Yawgmoth's ability targeting Grizzly Bears, [1] Bastion's drain trigger.
6 citations on the record
Step 2 of 11
All players pass; the top object - Bastion of Remembrance's drain trigger - resolves. This is drain #1.
Each of the four opponents loses 1 life: 40 -> 39 each. You gain 1 life: 39 -> 40. Opposing life in the pod: 160 -> 156.
2 citations on the record
Step 3 of 11
All players pass again; Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card.' Target: Grizzly Bears.
You, as the controller of the ability, follow its instructions in the order written: YOU put one -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, which becomes 1/1, and YOU draw a card (library 60 -> 59, hand 0 -> 1). The identity of the player placing the counter is the whole trigger condition of Nest of Scarabs, which reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens' - and because the ability's controller is you, that condition is met and Nest triggers for one counter. It has triggered and has done nothing yet - no token exists.
3 citations on the record
Step 4 of 11
A player would receive priority, so the game runs state-based actions first, repeats until none is performed, and only then puts waiting triggered abilities on the stack.
The state-based action check finds nothing to do: Grizzly Bears' toughness is 1, and the state-based action that moves a creature to its owner's graveyard applies only at toughness 0 or less (quoted at step 5, where it is met). Nest of Scarabs' trigger is then put on the stack and resolves, creating Insect token C under your control. ACTIVATION 1 COMPLETE. Bodies you control other than Yawgmoth: Insect B, Human Soldier token, Insect C = 3 (you spent Insect A and got Insect C: net zero, because the counter went onto an opponent's creature). You: 40 life, 59 cards in library, 1 card in hand. Opponents: 39 each.
1 citation on the record
Step 5 of 11
ACTIVATION 2, same shape: activate Yawgmoth targeting Grizzly Bears again, pay 1 life (40 -> 39), sacrifice Insect B. Bastion's trigger from Insect B's death resolves first (drain #2: opponents 39 -> 38, you 39 -> 40), then Yawgmoth's ability resolves, putting a second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears (now 0/0) and drawing a card.
Nest of Scarabs triggers again but has not resolved. Before that trigger can be put on the stack, state-based actions run: Grizzly Bears has toughness 0 and is put into its owner's graveyard - it dies. Bastion of Remembrance does NOT trigger for it: Bastion reads 'Whenever a creature you control dies', and the look-back the trigger performs (step 1) finds Grizzly Bears under opponent A's control at the moment it left the battlefield. Only then is Nest's trigger put on the stack; it resolves and creates Insect token D. This is the same ordering that refuted the near-infinite claim in stack 001: the Insect that REPLACES THE BODY YOU SPENT (Nest is a triggered ability, not a replacement effect - nothing here is replaced in the 614 sense) is created strictly after the death it makes up for, and it never exists while that iteration's target is being chosen. ACTIVATION 2 COMPLETE. Bodies other than Yawgmoth: Human Soldier, Insect C, Insect D = 3 (again net zero - the opponent's creature absorbed the counter). You: 40 life, library 58, 2 cards in hand. Opponents: 38 each.
3 citations on the record
Step 6 of 11
Census of legal targets for every subsequent activation, now that no opponent controls a creature. Targets for an activated ability are chosen as the ability is activated, from what exists at that moment.
Three candidates are ruled out and one class remains. (i) Yawgmoth himself is NEVER a legal target of his own ability: he has protection from Humans and he is a Human Cleric, so the ability's source has the stated quality and cannot target him. (ii) Bastion of Remembrance and Nest of Scarabs are Enchantments, not creatures, so they are not 'target creature' at all. (iii) The Insect that Nest of Scarabs will create does not exist when the target is chosen, so it can never be the target of the same activation that creates it. What remains: only the 1/1 tokens you already control - the Human Soldier token (a Human, but protection is a property of Yawgmoth, not a restriction on whom Yawgmoth may target) and any Insect tokens from earlier iterations. Every one of them dies to a single counter, which is what turns the engine body-negative at step 7.
2 citations on the record
Step 7 of 11
ACTIVATIONS 3 and 4 - the true engine iteration, where the counter must land on one of your own bodies. Activation 3: pay 1 life (40 -> 39), sacrifice Insect C, target Insect D. Activation 4: pay 1 life (41 -> 40), sacrifice the Human Soldier token, target Insect E.
Each such activation produces TWO Bastion drains, not one. First the sacrificed creature dies during cost payment (drain), and that trigger resolves before Yawgmoth's ability. Then Yawgmoth's ability resolves and puts a -1/-1 counter on your own 1/1, making it 0/0; Nest of Scarabs triggers. State-based actions then kill that 1/1 - a second creature you control dying, so Bastion triggers again (a token still triggers this before it ceases to exist). Bastion's new trigger and Nest's waiting trigger go on the stack together and you order them freely; both resolve. Net per iteration: minus 2 bodies (one sacrificed, one counter-killed) plus 1 Insect = NET MINUS ONE BODY; 2 drains minus the 1 life paid as a cost (the paying-life rule is cited at step 11, where the totals land) = NET PLUS 1 LIFE for you and MINUS 2 for each opponent. Activation 3 (drains #3, #4): opponents 38 -> 36, you 39 -> 41; bodies left = Human Soldier, Insect E = 2. Activation 4 (drains #5, #6): opponents 36 -> 34, you 40 -> 42; bodies left = Insect F = 1. Library 56, 4 cards in hand.
3 citations on the record
Step 8 of 11
ACTIVATION 5 - one body left (Insect F), and it is the same body the cost must eat. Targets are announced during the proposal of the ability and costs are paid afterward, so Insect F is still on the battlefield and IS a legal target choice when targets are declared. Two lines exist; take the better one.
Line A (target Insect F, then sacrifice it to pay the cost): on resolution the ability's only target is no longer in the zone it was in, every instance of 'target' is illegal, and the ability does not resolve - no counter, no Nest trigger, and NO CARD DRAWN. Line B (declare zero targets, which 'up to one target creature' expressly permits - an ability that allows zero targets to be chosen is not targeted at all when none is chosen, so the target-legality check on resolution never bites): the ability resolves, the counter instruction does nothing for want of a target, and you still draw a card; Nest of Scarabs does not trigger because no counter was put on anything, so no Insect replaces Insect F. Line B is strictly better and is the line taken. Either way Insect F's sacrifice gives exactly ONE Bastion drain (#7): pay 1 life (42 -> 41), drain back to 42; opponents 34 -> 33. ACTIVATION 5 COMPLETE. Library 55, 5 cards in hand. Bodies other than Yawgmoth: ZERO.
4 citations on the record
Step 9 of 11
Attempt a sixth activation of the SACRIFICE ability. The only creature on the battlefield is Yawgmoth himself, and his cost reads 'Sacrifice another creature'.
THE SACRIFICE LOOP IS BOUNDED AND IT ENDS HERE, on running out of creatures to sacrifice. You control no creature other than Yawgmoth, so the sacrifice cost is unpayable; partial payment is not allowed, and an activation begun and not legally completable is reversed in full with no abilities triggering. This exhausts the FIRST ability only - Yawgmoth's second ability, '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate', is still activatable here (three untapped Swamps, five cards in hand); in this line it would simply accomplish nothing, because no permanent and no player carries a counter once the Bears and every counter-bearing token are gone. Note precisely what did NOT end it: (i) not your life - you paid 5 life across five activations and gained 7, ending HIGHER than you started; (ii) not your library - 55 cards remain and the draw-from-an-empty-library loss never comes near; (iii) not the opponent's creature toughness - Grizzly Bears' 2 toughness only EXTENDED the engine, absorbing two counters and letting activations 1 and 2 cost zero net bodies; (iv) not running out of targets in the abstract, since your own Insects are always legal targets - you run out of bodies. The general rule for this board: completed activations = (sacrificeable bodies you start with) + (total toughness of opposing creatures you can put counters on) = 3 + 2 = 5.
3 citations on the record
Step 10 of 11
SEPARATE THE BOARD INVARIANT FROM THE LINE. Everything above is the sacrifice engine on its own; the board also offers Yawgmoth's SECOND ability, '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate', which the three Swamps can pay exactly once (they make {B}{B}{B}, and there is no second {B}{B} after that) and which has a card to discard from activation 1 onward. It does not change the number of completed sacrifice activations, and it does change the drain count.
BOARD INVARIANT (sacrifice engine alone, no proliferate): 5 completed activations, 7 Bastion triggers, 28 life removed from the pod - the figure reported in final_state. Sister stack 003 also lands on 28, but NOT on this board and NOT by this route, and the difference is the single most useful thing these two stacks teach together. 003 swaps BASTION OF REMEMBRANCE, an enchantment, for ZULAPORT CUTTHROAT, a 1/1 CREATURE, and has no Human Soldier token. That is forced: an enchantment cannot be a body, so this board's third sacrificeable body is the 1/1 white Human Soldier that Bastion's own enters-the-battlefield ability made, while on 003 the drainer IS the third body. Two consequences follow. (i) 003's drainer can die, and the Insect that Nest returns in its place cannot drain, so 003 caps at SEVEN drains even WITH a proliferate; this board's drainer is an enchantment that can never die, which is exactly why one proliferate here reaches EIGHT. (ii) The two 28s are not the same quantity reached the same way: 003's 28 is its ceiling INCLUDING one proliferate, while the 28 here is the NO-proliferate figure and this board still has 32 above it. The figures coincide only because both boards happen to offer three sacrificeable bodies and the same 2-toughness Grizzly Bears, so both run five activations - coincidence of arithmetic, not corroboration, and neither number checks the other. LINE-DEPENDENT VARIANT (one proliferate, taken after activation 1): proliferating the Grizzly Bears - the only permanent then carrying a counter - gives it a second -1/-1 counter. You, as the controller of that ability, are the one putting the counter, so Nest of Scarabs triggers for one Insect exactly as it does off Yawgmoth's first ability, and the Bears dies to a state-based action without any of your creatures being spent on it. That converts the Bears' remaining toughness into a BODY instead of into a free activation: the sacrifice-activation count is still FIVE, but one more iteration now lands its counter on your own creature, giving 8 Bastion drains instead of 7 (you 43, each opponent 32, 32 total drained, library 55, hand 4 after the discard). The two figures must not be blended: 7 / 28 is a property of the board, 8 / 32 is a property of a line that spends {B}{B} and a card.
2 citations on the record
Step 11 of 11
Final accounting over all five completed activations of the sacrifice ability (board-invariant line, no proliferate).
SEVEN creatures you controlled died: Insect A, Insect B, Insect C, the Human Soldier token and Insect F sacrificed as costs (5), plus Insect D and Insect E counter-killed by state-based action on activations 3 and 4 (2). Bastion of Remembrance therefore triggered SEVEN times, one per death. All seven were TOKENS, and the sequence is the mechanism that pays this deck, so state it in order rather than in reverse: each of the seven DID reach the graveyard - the five sacrifices are moved from the battlefield directly to their owner's graveyard by 701.21a (cited at step 1) and the two counter-killed Insects are put there by 704.5f (cited at steps 5 and 7) - and that arrival IS the 'dies' event of 700.4 (cited at step 5), which is the trigger condition every one of the seven Bastion drains keys on. Only afterwards, on the next state-based-action check (117.5, cited at steps 4 and 5), does 704.5d apply: each token is by then in a zone other than the battlefield and ceases to exist. It dies first and ceases to exist second. So the ending graveyard holds none of them and gains no card - graveyard_additions is empty as an END STATE, not because nothing of yours ever reached the graveyard. LIFE: you paid 1 life five times and gained 1 life seven times; the gain-and-lose adjustment for a Bastion drain is established at step 2 and every one of the seven drains has that identical shape. From the 40 you had before activation 1 (39 as the scenario states it, with the first payment already made) you finish at 42 - net +2 over the whole engine, +3 from the stated board. Per completed activation the net change to YOUR life is: 0 on activations 1, 2 and 5 (one drain, one life paid) and +1 on activations 3 and 4, which are the only iterations running in true engine mode (two drains, one life paid). Each of the four opponents loses exactly 7 life, 40 -> 33. Total life removed from the pod: 7 x 4 = 28, from 160 down to 132. LETHALITY, stated as a checked state-based action rather than an impression: the life-total check runs each time a player would receive priority (117.5, step 4) and never fires anywhere in this line - it takes a player at 0 or less life, and the lowest total on the table at any point is 33. Nobody is close to dead; this is a value engine and a sacrifice outlet, not a kill.
3 citations on the record

NOT unbounded. Reading the board as the scenario annotates it - Insect token A is already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability on the stack, so you have THREE sacrificeable bodies counting it (A, Insect B, Human Soldier token) - the engine is bounded by bodies. Once Grizzly Bears is dead, every legal target for Yawgmoth's first ability is one of your own 1/1 tokens (Yawgmoth cannot target himself through protection from Humans; the two enchantments are not creatures; the Insect that Nest of Scarabs will make does not exist when the target is chosen), so each iteration costs two bodies and returns one - net minus one body per activation. From this board there are exactly FIVE completed activations of the sacrifice ability, ended by having no other creature to sacrifice. The opponent's 2-toughness creature does not bound the engine, it EXTENDS it by two activations; your life goes UP, and your library is untouched at 55. THE BOARD'S NUMBER IS SEVEN: seven creatures you controlled die (five sacrificed, two counter-killed), Bastion of Remembrance triggers seven times, your life 39 -> 42, each opponent 40 -> 33, 28 total life removed from a pod of 160. Net life per activation for you is 0 while the counter goes onto the opponent's creature or nowhere, and +1 on the two true engine iterations. No opponent comes near losing: the lowest life total on the table is 33, so the life-total state-based action never fires (step 11). A LINE that also spends {B}{B} and a card on Yawgmoth's proliferate reaches 8 drains / 32 life removed / you at 43 without changing the five-activation count (step 10) - that is a property of the line, not of the board, and this final_state describes the board invariant.

Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Zulaport Cutthroat

does a creature drain survive its own engine?

Three permissions, and not a restriction among them. You choose which creature pays “Sacrifice another creature” (CR 601.2h), “up to one target” permits choosing none (CR 115.6), and a leaves-the-battlefield trigger looks back at the board as it was (CR 603.10a). Nothing here can take Zulaport Cutthroat away from you against your will. He survives every iteration and is spent as the deliberate final sacrifice, where his own trigger banks the seventh drain. The holding: seven drains, and the table lives through them.

Fix the reading first, then do the bookkeeping the stack picture omits. THE READING: the scenario's board annotates 'Insect token A' as already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack, matching the house convention used in stack 001 — the board list names the cost-payment body, it is NOT still on the battlefield, and the pilot's live insect count entering this resolution is ONE (Insect token B). Every number below is derived under that reading; the n=4 reading in which both A and B are alive is not the scenario as written. BOOKKEEPING: paying the cost sacrificed Insect token A, moving it from the battlefield to the graveyard, which is a 'dies' event. Zulaport Cutthroat's ability is a look-back-in-time trigger and sees that death even though Insect A is a TOKEN and a later state-based action removes it from the graveyard. That trigger is put on the stack ABOVE the activated ability and therefore resolves first.

Step 2

Zulaport Cutthroat's trigger resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' Four opponents, so four separate life losses.

Step 3

Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Before it does anything, its target is rechecked for legality: Grizzly Bears is still on the battlefield and nothing has changed its characteristics, so it is still a legal target and the ability resolves normally. Put one -1/-1 counter on it and draw a card.

4
Step 4

State-based actions are checked (nothing dies: Bears is 1/1), then Nest of Scarabs' trigger is put on the stack. All players pass priority in succession — the scenario's stated assumption — so it resolves, creating one 1/1 black Insect token (Insect C). This same all-pass condition is what lets every object that resolves across this main phase actually resolve, and it is not restated below.

5
Step 5

Answer (a), the load-bearing question: is the pilot ever FORCED to consume Zulaport Cutthroat? No — on three independent grounds. (i) Yawgmoth's ability is an ACTIVATED ability: the pilot may activate it, and is never compelled to, unlike stack 001's mandatory triggered ability. (ii) The cost is 'Sacrifice another creature': the pilot pays the cost and any creature they control other than Yawgmoth is a full payment, so an Insect token satisfies it whenever one exists. (iii) The effect is 'up to one target creature', so choosing ZERO targets is legal — the counter can never be forced onto Zulaport.

6
Step 6

Establish the loop's hard ceiling before running it. The engine is body-neutral only while some creature can absorb a -1/-1 counter and live: each activation spends one body (sacrifice) and Nest returns at most one body (one counter, one token). Every creature you control except Yawgmoth is a 1/1, so a counter on your own board kills the body it lands on. Yawgmoth is 2/4 and could absorb three counters — but he has protection from Humans and the source of his own activated ability is Yawgmoth, a Human Cleric, so he can never be targeted by it.

Step 7

Convert the three untapped Swamps — the resource the scenario deliberately supplies, since the -1/-1 ability costs no mana. Choose Grizzly Bears, the only permanent in the game with a counter on it, and give it a second -1/-1 counter.

8
Step 8

Activation #2 of the -1/-1 ability, and the step that puts the TOKEN-DEATH mechanic on the record. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect B (a token — it moves to the graveyard, which is the 'dies' event, and only ceases to exist afterwards), target Insect C. On resolution, put a -1/-1 counter on Insect C and draw. Insect C is now 0/0, and SBAs run as a single event: 704.5f puts Insect C into its owner's graveyard, and only on the NEXT SBA check — the repeat that 117.5 requires — is 704.5d applicable, because the token is only then in a zone other than the battlefield. That ordering is why each token death is a real 'dies' event Zulaport can see; a token that never reached the graveyard would produce one drain in this whole line, not seven. Two abilities have now triggered since the last priority — Zulaport's second death trigger and Nest of Scarabs' token trigger — and the pilot controls both, so the pilot chooses the order they go on the stack; both orders reach the same totals here, because Nest's replacement token can never become the target of an activation whose targets were already locked (step 4). This same simultaneous-trigger choice recurs in step 9.

9
Step 9

Activation #3, identical shape to step 8 in every respect — same sacrifice-death, same 704.5f-then-704.5d token disposal, same pilot-ordered simultaneous triggers, same life adjustments. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect D, target Insect E; the counter takes Insect E to 0/0 and it is put into the graveyard; Nest creates Insect F.

10
Step 10

Activation #4. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect F. Now the target choice matters: the only creatures left would be Zulaport Cutthroat and Yawgmoth. Yawgmoth cannot be targeted, and the pilot declines to kill Zulaport, so choose ZERO targets — legal because the ability reads 'up to one target creature'. With no target chosen there is nothing for the on-resolution legality check of step 3 to examine, so the ability resolves and simply draws.

11
Step 11

Activation #5, the final one, and the literal subject of the scenario's title: does the drain survive its own engine? Pay 1 life, sacrifice Zulaport Cutthroat itself. Its ability reads 'Whenever this creature or another creature you control dies' — a leaves-the-battlefield ability, and such abilities look back in time to the game state immediately before the event, so Zulaport sees the very sacrifice that removed it and its trigger goes on the stack. Zero targets again (only Yawgmoth remains and he is untargetable). Draw. Then the loop ENDS: Yawgmoth is the only creature you control and the cost demands ANOTHER creature, so activation #6 has no legal payment.

Optimal line: resolve the stacked activation, then spend {B}{B} + a discard on Yawgmoth's proliferate to put the second counter on Bears — that buys a free Insect token without spending a sacrifice, and is worth exactly one extra drain over using an activation's counter on Bears (7 vs 6). NOT LETHAL.

Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Blood Artist

can a single-target drain close a four-player table?

Four words decide the case. Blood Artist reads “target player loses 1 life” — one seat per trigger, chosen as the trigger goes on the stack — where the drains that end games read each opponent. The engine also finishes by eating its own payoff: protection bars the commander as a target and “another creature” bars him as the sacrifice, so the Artist is the last legal thing left to point at. The holding: eight triggers, eight life, against a pod holding 160. The arithmetic divides by four and never recovers.

Nothing has resolved yet; put the waiting triggered ability on the stack — but state-based actions go FIRST. Insect token A was sacrificed to pay Yawgmoth's activation cost, which moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, i.e. it died, and Blood Artist triggered on that death. No player had priority during the activation, so the trigger has been waiting. Each time a player would get priority the game performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event and repeats until none are performed, and ONLY THEN are waiting triggered abilities put on the stack.

STRUCTURAL FINDING — why the engine is body-negative once Grizzly Bears is gone. The cause is ARITHMETIC, not timing: the activation cost is one of your creatures and Nest of Scarabs returns exactly one Insect per counter placed, so the moment the counter also kills one of YOUR creatures, two of your creatures leave and one token arrives — net minus one. That holds identically even if the token appeared instantly; the token's lateness is a true but separate fact and is NOT the cause of the body loss.

Step 1 of 11
Nothing has resolved yet; put the waiting triggered ability on the stack — but state-based actions go FIRST. Insect token A was sacrificed to pay Yawgmoth's activation cost, which moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, i.e. it died, and Blood Artist triggered on that death. No player had priority during the activation, so the trigger has been waiting. Each time a player would get priority the game performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event and repeats until none are performed, and ONLY THEN are waiting triggered abilities put on the stack.
Order on this board: (i) Insect A is in the graveyard; (ii) SBA check — a token in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist, so Insect A CEASES TO EXIST here, before any trigger reaches the stack; (iii) the check is repeated, nothing further is performed, and Blood Artist's waiting trigger is then put on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability, its one target player chosen at that moment (targets are declared as part of putting an ability on the stack — cited at step 11, where that timing does the work). Insect A ceasing to exist does not undo the trigger — the ability had already triggered on the death. Stack, bottom to top: (pos 0) Yawgmoth's activated ability targeting Grizzly Bears; (pos 1) Blood Artist's trigger for Insect A's death, targeting opponent A. All players pass priority.

Step 3 of 11
Resolve pos 0: Yawgmoth's activated ability, putting a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and drawing a card.
Grizzly Bears becomes 1/1 and SURVIVES — this is the only creature on the table that can absorb a counter and live, and that fact is the entire engine. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59, hand 0 -> 1). Because YOU put a -1/-1 counter on a creature, Nest of Scarabs triggers — and the actor is established, not assumed: this is your activated ability and the controller of an ability is the player who follows its instructions, so the player putting the counter on is you. Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition is literally 'Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature', so the identity of that actor is the whole of the condition. State-based actions are checked first: Grizzly Bears at toughness 1 is not put into a graveyard, nothing else is illegal. Nest's trigger is then put on the stack and resolves: create Insect token C. Your Insects: B and C.
4 citations on the record
Step 4 of 11
STRUCTURAL FINDING — why the engine is body-negative once Grizzly Bears is gone. The cause is ARITHMETIC, not timing: the activation cost is one of your creatures and Nest of Scarabs returns exactly one Insect per counter placed, so the moment the counter also kills one of YOUR creatures, two of your creatures leave and one token arrives — net minus one. That holds identically even if the token appeared instantly; the token's lateness is a true but separate fact and is NOT the cause of the body loss.
What the timing does cost you is a target, not a body. Targets for an activated ability are chosen as the ability is ACTIVATED and the cost is paid later in the same process, so at the instant you choose a target (i) the creature you are about to sacrifice is still on the battlefield but will be gone at resolution, and (ii) the Insect that Nest is about to make does not exist yet and therefore can never be that same activation's target. Taxonomy for this board, stated exactly: an activation targeting one of YOUR 1/1 Insects (or the 0/1 Blood Artist) is minus one body and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears while it already holds one counter is BODY-NEUTRAL for you — you sacrifice one, Nest returns one, and the Bears is not your creature — and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears at 2/2 (it survives) is body-neutral and yields 1 drain; an activation choosing zero targets is minus one body, makes no token, and yields 1 drain. Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and step 3 has already spent it.
4 citations on the record
Step 5 of 11
Best line: spend Yawgmoth's SECOND ability now. Activate '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate' — tap two Swamps and discard the card you just drew. Grizzly Bears is the only permanent or player in the game with a counter on it, so it is the only legal proliferate choice, and it is chosen on resolution, not targeted.
Grizzly Bears gets a second -1/-1 counter and becomes 0/0. This is the non-obvious half of the Nest interaction, so state it in full: the definition of proliferate says what is done but never names who does it, and the actor comes from resolution instead — the controller of the ability follows its instructions, and you control this proliferate ability, so YOU are the player who puts that counter on. That is exactly Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition ('Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature'), so Nest of Scarabs triggers. State-based actions then bury Grizzly Bears, and Blood Artist triggers on its death (Blood Artist reads 'this creature or another creature dies' with no controller restriction, so an opponent's creature counts). Both triggers are yours and you order them. Resolve: Insect token D is created; DRAIN 2 — opponent B loses 1 life (40 -> 39), you gain 1 (40 -> 41). Your Insects: B, C, D. Mana left: one untapped Swamp. Hand: 0. Library: 59. The point of this activation is that its cost is mana and a card, NOT a creature: it removes the Bears and still makes an Insect without spending a body.

Step 6 of 11
ACTIVATION 2 of the sacrifice ability: pay 1 life (41 -> 40), sacrifice Insect B, target Insect C. (Targeting the very Insect you are sacrificing would be legal to announce, but at resolution that target is no longer where it was when targeted, so the ability would not resolve at all — which is why this mode needs two Insects on the battlefield.)
Blood Artist triggers on Insect B's death and goes on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability; it resolves first — DRAIN 3, opponent C 40 -> 39, you 40 -> 41. Yawgmoth's ability then resolves: -1/-1 counter on Insect C (1/1 -> 0/0), draw a card (library 58, hand 1). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Insect C for toughness 0, and Blood Artist triggers again — DRAIN 4, opponent D 40 -> 39, you 41 -> 42. Nest's trigger resolves: Insect token E. Net for the activation: two drains, one card drawn, and your Insect count fell from 3 to 2 (D and E).

1 citation on the record
Step 8 of 11
ACTIVATION 4 in this line — the one that consumes the payoff. With a single Insect left there is no second Insect to target, and Yawgmoth cannot be targeted by his own ability: he is a Human Cleric permanent, so his ability is an ability from a source with the stated quality and his protection from Humans forbids it. The only remaining legal creature target is Blood Artist itself. Pay 1 life (43 -> 42), sacrifice Insect F, target Blood Artist.
DRAIN 7 (Insect F dies): opponent C 39 -> 38, you 42 -> 43. Yawgmoth's ability resolves: a -1/-1 counter makes Blood Artist -1/0. Draw (library 56, hand 3). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Blood Artist for toughness 0. Blood Artist's ability is a leaves-the-battlefield ability, and leaves-the-battlefield abilities are among the zone-change triggers that LOOK BACK IN TIME. The mechanism is the general rule those exceptions hang off: for such an ability the game looks back in time to determine if it triggers, using the existence of those abilities and the appearance of objects immediately prior to the event. So Blood Artist sees its own death: DRAIN 8, opponent D 39 -> 38, you 43 -> 44. Nest's trigger resolves into Insect token G. Blood Artist is now in the graveyard and NO further death in this line drains anyone.

Step 9 of 11
ANSWER (a), part 1 — separate what the BOARD fixes from what the LINE chooses.
BOARD-LEVEL INVARIANT (the answer to 'how far does this board go'): a maximum of EIGHT creature deaths occur while Blood Artist is on the battlefield, hence EIGHT Blood Artist triggers and EIGHT life removed. That number is a property of the board and every optimal line reaches it. THE ACTIVATION COUNT IS LINE-DEPENDENT and is not a board property. Line 1, the one resolved in steps 1-8: four draining sacrifice activations plus one proliferate, after which a fifth sacrifice activation (sacrifice Insect G, zero targets — 'up to one target creature' permits zero, so the ability is not targeted at all) is legal but drains nobody because Blood Artist is already dead; that leaves Yawgmoth as your only creature and 'Sacrifice another creature' can never be paid again. Line 2, equally optimal: at step 8 instead sacrifice Insect F choosing ZERO targets (DRAIN 7; Blood Artist survives, no counter and so no Nest token), then as a fifth activation sacrifice BLOOD ARTIST itself with zero targets — its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees its death, DRAIN 8. That line runs FIVE draining sacrifice activations for the same eight drains, and ends at 43 life, library 55, with no Insect token G. So: 8 drains is the board's number; '4 draining activations' belongs to line 1 and '5' to line 2. ALTERNATIVES CHECKED AND REJECTED. (i) Skipping proliferate yields SEVEN drains, and the reason previously given — that killing the Bears with an activation 'costs a body' — is WITHDRAWN as false: that activation is body-NEUTRAL for you (sacrifice one Insect, Nest returns one, and the Bears was never yours). The true reason is that proliferate removes the Bears while spending no creature at all, so you enter the rest of the line one body richer, and on this board the remaining reach from any state is (drains so far) + 2 x (your Insects), because each Insect converts into its own death plus the death of whatever your counter kills. Proliferate branch: 2 + 2x3 = 8. Activation branch: 3 + 2x2 = 7. The extra body is worth 2 and the forgone activation only bought 1. (ii) Zero-target activations taken mid-line yield 1 drain instead of 2 for the same body, so they never beat targeting an Insect while a second Insect exists. (iii) Targeting the very creature you are sacrificing: the sacrifice puts it into the graveyard, so by the time the ability resolves it is no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted and the only target is illegal, so the ability does not resolve (and being a token it has by then ceased to exist as well — 704.5d, cited at step 1 — which is stronger than the illegality needs), costing you the counter, the Nest token AND the card draw, for one drain instead of two.

Step 10 of 11
ANSWER (a), part 2 — what ends it. Test each candidate bound named in the question.
NOT your library: line 1 draws 4 cards and 56 of 60 remain (line 2 draws 5 and leaves 55), so the deck-out state-based action is never approached. NOT your life: you pay 1 life per sacrifice activation and gain 1 life per drain, 4 paid against 8 gained, so you finish at 44 — HIGHER than you started. Life is a net gain and can never be the constraint while Blood Artist lives. NOT opposing toughness as a grind: Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and that window closes in step 3. What ends the engine is RUNNING OUT OF BODIES AND LEGAL TARGETS, by arithmetic: once Grizzly Bears is dead, every remaining creature target (a 1/1 Insect, the 0/1 Blood Artist) dies to a single counter, so such an activation removes two of your creatures while Nest returns one — net minus one. (Separately and truly: state-based actions bury the target before Nest's trigger is put on the stack, which is why the replacement Insect always arrives after the death it replaces and can never be that activation's target. That is a targeting fact, not the source of the minus one.) Yawgmoth's protection from Humans forbids him as a target and 'another creature' forbids him as the sacrifice, so the last legal target is Blood Artist and the engine's final act is eating its own payoff. This is a strictly finite chain, not a loop with no way to stop, so no draw question arises.

Step 11 of 11
ANSWER (b) — total reach across the pod, and the straight yes/no.
EIGHT life total is removed from the opposing side, out of 160. Blood Artist's ability says 'target player loses 1 life' — one instance of the word target, singular — and a triggered ability's targets are declared when it is put on the stack, by the same announcement process a spell uses. Each of the eight triggers is therefore a SEPARATE ability object making its own single choice, so the eight may be spread or all aimed at one player, but no single trigger can ever reach more than one. Eight creature deaths occurred while Blood Artist was on the battlefield (Insect A, Grizzly Bears, Insect B, Insect C, Insect D, Insect E, Insect F, Blood Artist itself), so eight triggers and eight points of life loss. Spread evenly the pilot takes 2 from each opponent: 38 / 38 / 38 / 38. Concentrated on one player it takes that player from 40 to 32 and leaves the other three at 40. NO — this line cannot reduce all four opponents from 40 to 0. It falls short by 152, and the requirement is not a few more iterations but 160 creature deaths with Blood Artist on the battlefield against the 8 this board produces. The single-target wording is doing a fraction of the work of 'each opponent loses 1 life', but the substitute figure is NOT one number, and the reason is BODIES rather than the Bears. This board holds three creature bodies: Blood Artist, Insect A (already sacrificed to pay the cost) and Insect B. Both substitutes read 'creature you control' — Zulaport Cutthroat 'this creature or another creature you control dies', Bastion of Remembrance 'a creature you control dies' — so both miss Grizzly Bears, opponent A's creature, identically; that is the same one death for each and it is not what separates them. ZULAPORT CUTTHROAT IS ITSELF A CREATURE. Substituted into Blood Artist's slot it preserves all three bodies, so the terminal activation still has a legal creature target (Zulaport itself), Zulaport dies as the engine's final act and its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees that death: SEVEN deaths of creatures you control, the seventh being the drainer itself, and 7 x 4 = 28 life removed. Penalty against Blood Artist's 8: 3.5x. BASTION OF REMEMBRANCE IS AN ENCHANTMENT AND NEVER DIES. Substituted into that same slot it REMOVES a body and leaves two, so the terminal activation has no legal creature target at all — Yawgmoth is unreachable through protection from Humans — it takes zero targets, Bastion is never on the receiving end of anything, and only Insects A through F die: SIX deaths of creatures you control, and 6 x 4 = 24 life removed. Penalty: 3.0x. SISTER-STACK RECONCILIATION, stated LIKE-FOR-LIKE so this does not read as self-contradiction. Both substitution figures above are computed on a WITH-proliferate line (the proliferate of step 5), so each may only be set against a with-proliferate figure. Stack 003's board is identical to this one but for the drainer slot and it also runs the proliferate, so its 28 for Zulaport IS the 28 reached here — like-for-like, and it stands. Stack 002's headline 28 is a DIFFERENT QUANTITY: it is 002's NO-proliferate board invariant, and 002 carries a separate proliferate_variant reporting 8 deaths of creatures you control, 32 life removed, each opponent at 32 and you at 43, with the explicit instruction that the two are never blended. So the like-for-like Bastion comparison is this board's 24 against 002's 32, NOT against 002's 28. The whole of that 8-life gap is ONE BODY: 002's board carries a 1/1 white Human Soldier token — the very token Bastion's own enters-the-battlefield ability creates — which this board does not, so 002 runs its with-proliferate line from three creature bodies (Insect A, Insect B, the Soldier) where this board has two (Insect A, Insect B). Under step 9's own rule an extra body on this engine is worth TWO deaths, its own plus the death of whatever its counter kills, so the delta is +2 deaths and +2 x 4 = +8 life: 6 deaths / 24 life here, 8 deaths / 32 life there. The +2 holds exactly even though a Bastion line's TERMINAL body yields only one death (with no creature drainer to receive the last counter, the final activation takes zero targets), because both boards pay that same terminal penalty once. It is a two-death delta, not the one-death 'restored seventh death' asserted in the previous iteration, which is withdrawn as false. Neither figure is a correction to 002. Without the proliferate the Blood Artist figure is 7, distributed 2 / 2 / 2 / 1, and the answer is the same NO.
3 citations on the record

NOT A KILL, AND NOT EVEN CLOSE — a further refutation of the 'near-infinite' family on this deck, for a different reason than stack 001. This file claims NO ordinal: sister stack 003 holds the 'second time' on this deck and this refutation comes after it. The Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs engine is body-neutral only while the creature taking the -1/-1 counter either survives it or is not yours. On this board exactly one creature survives a counter: Grizzly Bears, toughness 2, and its single survivable counter is spent by the ability already on the stack. After that every legal target is a 1/1 Insect or the 0/1 Blood Artist, and killing one of your own creatures makes the activation minus one body by plain arithmetic — two of your creatures leave, Nest returns one. (The replacement token also arrives after the death, since state-based actions run before waiting triggers reach the stack; that is why it can never be the same activation's target, but it is not what makes the engine body-negative.) Yawgmoth's protection from Humans bars him as a target and 'another creature' bars him as the sacrifice, so the last legal target is Blood Artist itself and the engine ends by eating its own payoff. THE BOARD'S NUMBER IS EIGHT: eight creature deaths with Blood Artist on the battlefield, eight triggers, eight life removed, and exhaustive line-space search finds nothing better. The ACTIVATION COUNT is line-dependent and is not a property of the board: the line resolved here runs four draining sacrifice activations plus one proliferate (and a fifth, non-draining sacrifice activation is then available), while an equally optimal zero-target line runs FIVE draining sacrifice activations for the same eight drains, ending at 43 life with 55 cards and no Insect token G. The proliferate is worth exactly one extra drain because its cost is mana and a card rather than a creature: it removes the Bears while spending no body, and on this board remaining reach is (drains so far) + 2 x (your Insects). Without it the figure is seven. The engine is bounded by bodies and legal targets — not by your library (56 of 60 remain in the line shown), not by your life (40 -> 44, a net gain), and not by any mandatory loop, because the chain terminates on its own. Total reach: 8 life out of 160, spread 2 / 2 / 2 / 2 or concentrated for 40 -> 32 on one player. The answer to (b) is NO, short by 152. The single-target penalty is two different factors, and the split is about BODIES, not about Grizzly Bears: both substitutes read 'creature you control' and both miss the Bears identically. This board holds three creature bodies (Blood Artist, Insect A, Insect B). Zulaport Cutthroat IS a creature, so swapping it in keeps three bodies, it dies as the engine's final act and sees its own death — seven own-creature deaths, 7 x 4 = 28 life, a 3.5x penalty, and the same 28 sister stack 003 reaches on a board of this shape. Bastion of Remembrance is an ENCHANTMENT and never dies, so swapping it in removes a body: the terminal activation has no legal creature target, takes zero targets, and only six own creatures die — 6 x 4 = 24 life, a 3.0x penalty. Both substitution figures are WITH-proliferate and therefore compare only against with-proliferate figures. 003's Zulaport 28 is like-for-like — identical board but for the drainer slot, proliferate also taken — and agrees exactly. Sister stack 002's headline 28 is its NO-proliferate BOARD INVARIANT; 002's comparable figure is its own proliferate_variant of 8 deaths / 32 life / opponents at 32, and 002 states the two are never blended — so the like-for-like Bastion comparison is 24 against 32, not against 28. That 8-life gap is exactly ONE BODY: 002's board carries a 1/1 white Human Soldier token (the token Bastion's own ETB makes) that this board does not, so 002 runs three creature bodies to this board's two, and by step 9's own rule a body is worth +2 deaths / +8 life — 6 deaths / 24 here becomes 8 deaths / 32 there. It is a two-death delta, not a one-death 'restored seventh death'. Neither figure corrects 002. Deckbuilding consequence: Blood Artist is a reach card only against ONE player, and the Yawgmoth engine needs a high-toughness creature (or a recursive body) to be more than a five-death burst — no amount of table presence fixes a drain that divides by four.

Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + South Wind Avatar

a lifegain-triggered drain, and a 4-toughness body

CR 119.9 is the clause, and it is a small one: an event that gains you 0 life is not a life-gain event at all. An Insect killed by its own -1/-1 counter dies at toughness 0, so South Wind Avatar's dies-trigger resolves for nothing and the second ability never fires. The holding: eight dies-triggers, seven drains. The Avatar's 4 toughness is what does the real work here, absorbing counters that a 1/1 drainer could not take.

The board and the walk-through — 12 step(s)

1
Step 1

Account for the cost that has already been paid. The scenario's board list annotates Insect token A as "already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack", so A is NOT on the battlefield as this resolution begins: Yawgmoth's ability was activated by paying 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and sacrificing Insect token A. Sacrificing A moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, so A died, and South Wind Avatar's first ability ('Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness') triggered on that cost payment. Stated explicitly so nothing is left to inference: every number below is computed from that reading of the board, which is the same house convention this deck's stack 001 uses for a sacrifice paid as a cost.

2
Step 2

Fix the targets and the permanent targeting constraint. Yawgmoth's ability reads 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card.' Its target (Grizzly Bears) was locked in as the ability was activated, not on resolution - and 115.1c points at 602.2b, which makes the whole 601.2b-i announcement sequence apply to activating an ability. Yawgmoth himself has protection from Humans and is the source of a Human's ability, so he is never a legal target for it.

3
Step 3

Resolve the top object: South Wind Avatar's lifegain trigger for Insect token A. This is a leaves-the-battlefield/dies trigger, one of the trigger classes that looks back in time, and 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so the value comes from last known information. Insect A carried NO -1/-1 counter - it was sacrificed as a cost, not killed by a counter - so its last known toughness on the battlefield was 1.

4
Step 4

The life gain is a real (nonzero) life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's second ability ('Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life') triggers. Cited here rather than assumed: an ability written 'whenever you gain life' is treated as though it read 'whenever a source causes you to gain life', so it keys on the EVENT and fires once per gain, whatever the size of the gain. It goes on the stack and resolves.

5
Step 5

Resolve Yawgmoth's activated ability: put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness, making Grizzly Bears 1/1. Nest of Scarabs reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens', and that condition has two halves, both of which must be shown. (i) A counter is put on a creature: Grizzly Bears is a creature, and the trigger is unrestricted as to whose creature it is, so an opponent's creature counts. (ii) YOU put it: the counter is placed by a resolving activated ability you control, and it is the controller of an ability who follows its instructions, so you are the player putting the counter. Both halves are met, so Nest triggers (trigger matching per CR 603.2, cited at step 4). Then resolve that Nest of Scarabs trigger, the next object on the stack: create one 1/1 black Insect creature token (call it C).

6
Step 6

ACTIVATION 2. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect B - it has no counters, so its last known toughness is 1: the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40), which drains each opponent for 1 (opponents 38 each). The ability resolves putting the second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, making it 0/0. Before any player gets priority, state-based actions run first and put it in the graveyard; only then do triggered abilities go on the stack.

7
Step 7

THE CRUX - what an Insect's toughness actually is when a -1/-1 counter kills it. ACTIVATIONS 3, 4 and 5 each sacrifice a counterless Insect (the Avatar gains you 1, each opponent loses 1) and put counters 1, 2 and 3 on South Wind Avatar - a 3/4, and -1/-1 counters subtract from POWER as well as toughness (122.1a, cited at step 5), so it goes 2/3, then 1/2, then 0/1, never dying, because a 4-toughness body absorbs 3 counters and is still alive at 1 toughness - each counter making one replacement Insect. ACTIVATION 6 has THREE legal targets at announcement - South Wind Avatar, still alive at 0/1 with three counters, and BOTH Insects then on the battlefield, F and G. F counts precisely because targets are announced at 601.2c before the cost is paid at 601.2h (step 9): F is still on the battlefield when targets are chosen, so it is a legal target, even though paying the cost is about to sacrifice it. Targeting F is NOT self-defeating, and the reason step 9 gives for declining to target Insect J does not carry here. Step 9's argument works only because J is at that point the ONLY sacrificeable body, so targeting it forces the cost to sacrifice the very permanent it targeted. At activation 6 there are two counterless Insects, and the sacrifice is chosen at 601.2h - AFTER targets are announced at 601.2c (step 9) - so targeting F simply means you pay the cost with G instead. F and G are interchangeable counterless 1/1 tokens, so the two options are EQUIVALENT by F-G symmetry, not one good and one self-defeating. Activation 6's real choice is therefore binary: spend the counter on an Insect (F or G, identically) or on the Avatar, and it is decided on other grounds - the Avatar line is already costed below. The best line spends the counter on one of your own Insects, say G, sacrificing F. It is not compelled to. (The alternative is legal and instructive: spending the Avatar's fourth counter here still reaches 8 activations, but it retires the drain two activations early and yields only 6 drains, 24 opposing life instead of 28. That choice is where 4 points of drain live.) The targeted Insect becomes 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by the same state-based action that killed Grizzly Bears at step 6 (CR 704.5f), and - being a token - it then ceases to exist. The Avatar's first ability DOES trigger on that death and DOES resolve; but 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so it is read from last known information, and the Insect's last known toughness ON the battlefield was 0, not 1, because the counter was already on it. TRIGGER ORDERING, since two of your triggered abilities are waiting at that same moment: Nest of Scarabs (triggered mid-resolution, when the counter was placed) and the Avatar's dies-trigger (triggered during the state-based action check that killed the Insect). Neither has 'another ability triggering' as its trigger condition, so both go on the stack together in the first part of the 603.3b process, in whichever order you choose. The order is outcome-neutral here: the Nest trigger creates its token either way, and the Avatar's trigger gains 0 either way.

8
Step 8

ACTIVATION 7 - the Avatar's fourth toughness point, and its own death. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect H (counterless, toughness 1): the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40) and each opponent loses 1 (opponents 33 each). Critically, the Avatar is still on the battlefield when that sacrifice happens - the cost is paid before the ability resolves - so this drain is collected in full. The ability then resolves, putting the FOURTH -1/-1 counter on South Wind Avatar, making it -1/0. State-based actions put it into the graveyard.

9
Step 9

ACTIVATION 8 and the terminus, narrated in the order the rules actually run. Activating an ability follows the 601.2 sequence (step 2's 115.1c points at 602.2b for exactly this), and in that sequence targets are ANNOUNCED at 601.2c and the total cost is paid five lettered steps later at 601.2h, with 601.2d (dividing or distributing an effect), 601.2e (the legality check), 601.2f (determining the total cost) and 601.2g (activating mana abilities) all intervening. The only thing the argument needs is the DIRECTION - announcement precedes payment - and that direction is what 601.2c and 601.2h jointly fix. So at the moment targets are chosen for activation 8, Insect J is still on the battlefield and IS a legal target; Yawgmoth is not (step 2). Choosing zero targets is therefore a CHOICE the ability permits ('up to one target creature'), not a compulsion forced by an empty target pool. It is the correct choice: had J been targeted, paying the cost would then have sacrificed J, moving it off the battlefield (step 1's 701.21a), and on resolution the ability's only target would have been illegal - so the ability would not have resolved at all and you would have lost the card. With no target chosen, the cost is paid: 1 life (you 39) and Insect J sacrificed. No Avatar is on the battlefield, so no lifegain trigger and no drain. The ability resolves; no counter is placed, so Nest of Scarabs does not trigger and no replacement Insect is created; you draw a card (library 53 -> 52).

10
Step 10

DISPOSE OF PROLIFERATE - Yawgmoth's OTHER activated ability, which the scenario's mana line invites. Yawgmoth, Thran Physician also reads '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate.' The scenario grants {B}{B}{B} from three untapped Swamps, which the first ability never spends, and the hand starts empty but holds a card from activation 1's draw onward - so exactly ONE proliferate is available in this sequence (three Swamps cannot make a second {B}{B}). To proliferate is to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter and give each one more counter of each kind it already has. What that can reach here: Grizzly Bears while it carries one counter, and South Wind Avatar once it carries counters. It can NEVER reach one of your Insects - a 1/1 Insect that takes a -1/-1 counter is 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by a state-based action before any player receives priority to activate anything (step 6's 704.5f), so no Insect ever bears a counter at a time when proliferate could be activated. No player here has poison or any other counter.

11
Step 11

PROVE THE BOUND, by case exhaustion over how the Avatar leaves the battlefield - and answer (b). (i) THE BODY LEDGER. Every completed activation consumes exactly one creature other than Yawgmoth (the sacrifice) and returns exactly one Insect for each -1/-1 counter placed on a creature. A counter placed on one of YOUR Insects is body-neutral: +1 token, -1 Insect (step 7). A counter placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar adds a body without removing one of yours - except the counter that finally kills the Avatar, which removes the Avatar itself as a sacrificeable body. Absorption is capped by printed toughness: Bears 2, Avatar 4. So the total number of creatures you can ever sacrifice is 3 (Insect A, Insect B, the Avatar) + (counters on Bears, at most 2) + (counters on the Avatar, at most 4) - (1 if the Avatar's counters killed it). Enumerate the cases: [A] the Avatar takes 4 counters and dies to them - 3 + 2 + 4 - 1 = 8; [B] the Avatar takes at most 3 counters and is SACRIFICED instead - 3 + 2 + 3 - 0 = 8; [C] the Avatar neither dies to a counter nor is sacrificed - it is then not a sacrificeable body at all, giving 2 + 2 + 3 = 7. No case exceeds 8, and steps 1-9 attain 8, so the bound is tight at 8. (This replaces the earlier claim that an eighth drain 'would require the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice, which caps activations at 7' - that reasoning is false, and case [B] is the counterexample: leave the Avatar on three counters and sacrifice it as activation 8 and you have 8 activations with the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice.) (ii) THE DRAIN CEILING IS 7, and the correct reason has two parts. A drain requires a nonzero lifegain event, which requires a creature you control to die with last known toughness at least 1 while the Avatar is on the battlefield; the only deaths that qualify are sacrifices of counterless Insects, because a counter-killed Insect dies at toughness 0 (step 7), Grizzly Bears is not yours (step 6), and the Avatar's own death never triggers its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). So drains are at most one per sacrifice, at most 8. That 8 is unreachable, and the proof is by SUPPLY OF BODIES - not by any claim that an 8-activation line sacrifices everything it has, which is FALSE: Insect G is consumed by a counter-kill and never sacrificed at all, which is the whole content of step 7. Suppose instead, for contradiction, that all 8 sacrifices drained. Each would then have to be the sacrifice of a counterless INSECT made while the Avatar was still on the battlefield - the Avatar itself can never be one of them, since its own death does not trigger its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). Now count how many Insect bodies can exist under that supposition. You start with 2 (A and B). Every further Insect is a Nest token, one per -1/-1 counter placed, and a counter placed on one of your own Insects is exactly body-neutral - it kills one and creates one (step 7) - so only counters placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar can grow the supply. Bears absorbs at most 2. The Avatar can have absorbed at most 3 while still being on the battlefield, because a fourth counter kills it (step 8) and the supposition requires it alive at every one of the 8 sacrifices. That is at most 2 + 3 = 5 supply-growing counters, hence at most 5 tokens, hence at most 2 + 5 = 7 Insect bodies exist in time to be sacrificed - one short of the 8 the supposition demands. Contradiction, so at most 7 drains. The count is unchanged if proliferate places some of those counters (step 10), since they come out of the same fixed 2 + 4 absorption pool. The case split says the same thing concretely: in case [B] the Avatar leaves by being sacrificed and that sacrifice is its own death, which does not trigger it; in case [A] it is counter-killed, and the Nest token made by the killing counter is created after the Avatar is already in the graveyard (step 8) - a body that, being a token from a counter-kill rather than the last body standing, still has to be disposed of with no Avatar on the battlefield. Either way at least one of the 8 sacrifices is drainless, which is what the supply count proves in general - and the line in steps 1-9 attains 7, simultaneously with 8 activations.

12
Step 12

Tally. South Wind Avatar's first ability triggered 8 times over the run: seven sacrifices of counterless 1/1 Insects (last known toughness 1, so 1 life each) plus one counter-killed Insect at toughness 0 (0 life). Its second ability triggered 7 times, once per nonzero lifegain event, draining 1 from each of four opponents each time.

Resolve
Step 1 of 12
Account for the cost that has already been paid. The scenario's board list annotates Insect token A as "already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack", so A is NOT on the battlefield as this resolution begins: Yawgmoth's ability was activated by paying 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and sacrificing Insect token A. Sacrificing A moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, so A died, and South Wind Avatar's first ability ('Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness') triggered on that cost payment. Stated explicitly so nothing is left to inference: every number below is computed from that reading of the board, which is the same house convention this deck's stack 001 uses for a sacrifice paid as a cost.
The Avatar's death trigger is waiting to be put on the stack. It goes on the stack the next time a player would receive priority - which is immediately after activation finishes - so it sits ABOVE Yawgmoth's activated ability and will resolve FIRST. The scenario's stack listing (one object) is therefore incomplete: the true stack is [pos 0: Yawgmoth's ability, pos 1: Avatar lifegain trigger].

Its target (Grizzly Bears) was locked in as the ability was activated, not on resolution - and 115.1c points at 602.2b, which makes the whole 601.2b-i announcement sequence apply to activating an ability. Yawgmoth himself has protection from Humans and is the source of a Human's ability, so he is never a legal target for it.
Grizzly Bears is the target for this activation. For every later activation, the legal target pool is: Grizzly Bears (while alive), South Wind Avatar (while alive), and Insect tokens on the battlefield at the moment targets are announced - never Yawgmoth. Nest of Scarabs is an enchantment, not a creature, so it is never a target either. The supply of creatures that can absorb a counter without costing you a body is what ultimately ends the engine.

Insect A carried NO -1/-1 counter - it was sacrificed as a cost, not killed by a counter - so its last known toughness on the battlefield was 1.
You gain 1 life. Life: you 39 -> 40.

Step 4 of 12
The life gain is a real (nonzero) life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's second ability ('Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life') triggers. Cited here rather than assumed: an ability written 'whenever you gain life' is treated as though it read 'whenever a source causes you to gain life', so it keys on the EVENT and fires once per gain, whatever the size of the gain. It goes on the stack and resolves.
Each of the four opponents loses 1 life. Opponents: 40 -> 39 each. Four life lost in total from this one drain - 1 per opponent per lifegain event, never 1 per point of life gained.

Step 5 of 12
Resolve Yawgmoth's activated ability: put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness, making Grizzly Bears 1/1. Nest of Scarabs reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens', and that condition has two halves, both of which must be shown. (i) A counter is put on a creature: Grizzly Bears is a creature, and the trigger is unrestricted as to whose creature it is, so an opponent's creature counts. (ii) YOU put it: the counter is placed by a resolving activated ability you control, and it is the controller of an ability who follows its instructions, so you are the player putting the counter. Both halves are met, so Nest triggers (trigger matching per CR 603.2, cited at step 4). Then resolve that Nest of Scarabs trigger, the next object on the stack: create one 1/1 black Insect creature token (call it C).
Grizzly Bears is 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59). Nest of Scarabs triggered once, for one counter, and that trigger resolves into Insect C. ACTIVATION 1 COMPLETE: you control Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs, South Wind Avatar (3/4), Insect B and Insect C. Body count is NET NEUTRAL - one Insect was consumed as a cost and one was created by the counter - and life is net neutral too (paid 1, gained 1). This is why the engine is a loop rather than a one-shot, and why neither life nor the library is what bounds it. The bound is the supply of -1/-1 counter targets that can absorb a counter without costing you a body.

Step 6 of 12
ACTIVATION 2. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect B - it has no counters, so its last known toughness is 1: the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40), which drains each opponent for 1 (opponents 38 each). The ability resolves putting the second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, making it 0/0. Before any player gets priority, state-based actions run first and put it in the graveyard; only then do triggered abilities go on the stack.
Grizzly Bears dies. It is an OPPONENT'S creature, so South Wind Avatar's 'another creature YOU CONTROL dies' ability does not trigger for it - no life, no drain from its death. The Nest of Scarabs trigger from the counter still happened at the moment the counter was placed and is unaffected by the subsequent death: Insect D is created. Draw (library 58). Insects: C, D. Grizzly Bears has now absorbed its full 2 toughness worth of counters - 2 activations' worth of replacement Insects.
2 citations on the record
Step 7 of 12
THE CRUX - what an Insect's toughness actually is when a -1/-1 counter kills it. ACTIVATIONS 3, 4 and 5 each sacrifice a counterless Insect (the Avatar gains you 1, each opponent loses 1) and put counters 1, 2 and 3 on South Wind Avatar - a 3/4, and -1/-1 counters subtract from POWER as well as toughness (122.1a, cited at step 5), so it goes 2/3, then 1/2, then 0/1, never dying, because a 4-toughness body absorbs 3 counters and is still alive at 1 toughness - each counter making one replacement Insect. ACTIVATION 6 has THREE legal targets at announcement - South Wind Avatar, still alive at 0/1 with three counters, and BOTH Insects then on the battlefield, F and G. F counts precisely because targets are announced at 601.2c before the cost is paid at 601.2h (step 9): F is still on the battlefield when targets are chosen, so it is a legal target, even though paying the cost is about to sacrifice it. Targeting F is NOT self-defeating, and the reason step 9 gives for declining to target Insect J does not carry here. Step 9's argument works only because J is at that point the ONLY sacrificeable body, so targeting it forces the cost to sacrifice the very permanent it targeted. At activation 6 there are two counterless Insects, and the sacrifice is chosen at 601.2h - AFTER targets are announced at 601.2c (step 9) - so targeting F simply means you pay the cost with G instead. F and G are interchangeable counterless 1/1 tokens, so the two options are EQUIVALENT by F-G symmetry, not one good and one self-defeating. Activation 6's real choice is therefore binary: spend the counter on an Insect (F or G, identically) or on the Avatar, and it is decided on other grounds - the Avatar line is already costed below. The best line spends the counter on one of your own Insects, say G, sacrificing F. It is not compelled to. (The alternative is legal and instructive: spending the Avatar's fourth counter here still reaches 8 activations, but it retires the drain two activations early and yields only 6 drains, 24 opposing life instead of 28. That choice is where 4 points of drain live.) The targeted Insect becomes 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by the same state-based action that killed Grizzly Bears at step 6 (CR 704.5f), and - being a token - it then ceases to exist. The Avatar's first ability DOES trigger on that death and DOES resolve; but 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so it is read from last known information, and the Insect's last known toughness ON the battlefield was 0, not 1, because the counter was already on it. TRIGGER ORDERING, since two of your triggered abilities are waiting at that same moment: Nest of Scarabs (triggered mid-resolution, when the counter was placed) and the Avatar's dies-trigger (triggered during the state-based action check that killed the Insect). Neither has 'another ability triggering' as its trigger condition, so both go on the stack together in the first part of the 603.3b process, in whichever order you choose. The order is outcome-neutral here: the Nest trigger creates its token either way, and the Avatar's trigger gains 0 either way.
You gain 0 life from that death. Gaining 0 life is not a life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's SECOND ability does not trigger at all: no opponent loses life for that Insect. Stated plainly - a counter-killed Insect is worth 0 life and 0 drain; only a SACRIFICED (counterless) Insect, last known toughness 1, is worth 1 life and 4 opposing life. Exactly one death in the optimal line is of the counter-killed kind. Running totals after activation 6: opponents 34 each, you 40, library 54, Insects: one (H). South Wind Avatar is 0/1 with three counters (a 3/4 reduced by three -1/-1 counters, power and toughness alike).
