A-004 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + Blood Artist ✓ cleared, 4 cycle(s) · 11 steps · 32 citations
Rules version June 19, 2026
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) Blood Artist's trigger reads 'target player loses 1 life and you gain 1 life' — a single target chosen per trigger. State the exact number of completed activations from this board and what ends it. (b) Given that each trigger drains only ONE player, how much life can be removed in total, and can this line reduce all four opponents from 40 to 0?
  1. Nothing has resolved yet; put the waiting triggered ability on the stack — but state-based actions go FIRST. Insect token A was sacrificed to pay Yawgmoth's activation cost, which moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, i.e. it died, and Blood Artist triggered on that death. No player had priority during the activation, so the trigger has been waiting. Each time a player would get priority the game performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event and repeats until none are performed, and ONLY THEN are waiting triggered abilities put on the stack.
    Order on this board: (i) Insect A is in the graveyard; (ii) SBA check — a token in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist, so Insect A CEASES TO EXIST here, before any trigger reaches the stack; (iii) the check is repeated, nothing further is performed, and Blood Artist's waiting trigger is then put on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability, its one target player chosen at that moment (targets are declared as part of putting an ability on the stack — cited at step 11, where that timing does the work). Insect A ceasing to exist does not undo the trigger — the ability had already triggered on the death. Stack, bottom to top: (pos 0) Yawgmoth's activated ability targeting Grizzly Bears; (pos 1) Blood Artist's trigger for Insect A's death, targeting opponent A. All players pass priority.
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  2. Resolve the top object: Blood Artist's trigger for Insect A's death.
    DRAIN 1. Opponent A loses 1 life (40 -> 39). You gain 1 life (39 -> 40). Note the shape that decides this whole scenario: ONE player lost 1 life, not each opponent. Opponents B, C and D are untouched.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
  3. Resolve pos 0: Yawgmoth's activated ability, putting a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and drawing a card.
    Grizzly Bears becomes 1/1 and SURVIVES — this is the only creature on the table that can absorb a counter and live, and that fact is the entire engine. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59, hand 0 -> 1). Because YOU put a -1/-1 counter on a creature, Nest of Scarabs triggers — and the actor is established, not assumed: this is your activated ability and the controller of an ability is the player who follows its instructions, so the player putting the counter on is you. Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition is literally 'Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature', so the identity of that actor is the whole of the condition. State-based actions are checked first: Grizzly Bears at toughness 1 is not put into a graveyard, nothing else is illegal. Nest's trigger is then put on the stack and resolves: create Insect token C. Your Insects: B and C.
    CR 122.1a — “Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  4. STRUCTURAL FINDING — why the engine is body-negative once Grizzly Bears is gone. The cause is ARITHMETIC, not timing: the activation cost is one of your creatures and Nest of Scarabs returns exactly one Insect per counter placed, so the moment the counter also kills one of YOUR creatures, two of your creatures leave and one token arrives — net minus one. That holds identically even if the token appeared instantly; the token's lateness is a true but separate fact and is NOT the cause of the body loss.
    What the timing does cost you is a target, not a body. Targets for an activated ability are chosen as the ability is ACTIVATED and the cost is paid later in the same process, so at the instant you choose a target (i) the creature you are about to sacrifice is still on the battlefield but will be gone at resolution, and (ii) the Insect that Nest is about to make does not exist yet and therefore can never be that same activation's target. Taxonomy for this board, stated exactly: an activation targeting one of YOUR 1/1 Insects (or the 0/1 Blood Artist) is minus one body and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears while it already holds one counter is BODY-NEUTRAL for you — you sacrifice one, Nest returns one, and the Bears is not your creature — and yields 2 drains; an activation targeting Grizzly Bears at 2/2 (it survives) is body-neutral and yields 1 drain; an activation choosing zero targets is minus one body, makes no token, and yields 1 drain. Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and step 3 has already spent it.
    CR 115.1c — “The target(s) are chosen as the ability is activated; see rule 602.2b.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2b–i.”
    CR 601.2c — “The player announces their choice of an appropriate object or player for each target the spell requires.”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
  5. Best line: spend Yawgmoth's SECOND ability now. Activate '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate' — tap two Swamps and discard the card you just drew. Grizzly Bears is the only permanent or player in the game with a counter on it, so it is the only legal proliferate choice, and it is chosen on resolution, not targeted.
    Grizzly Bears gets a second -1/-1 counter and becomes 0/0. This is the non-obvious half of the Nest interaction, so state it in full: the definition of proliferate says what is done but never names who does it, and the actor comes from resolution instead — the controller of the ability follows its instructions, and you control this proliferate ability, so YOU are the player who puts that counter on. That is exactly Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition ('Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature'), so Nest of Scarabs triggers. State-based actions then bury Grizzly Bears, and Blood Artist triggers on its death (Blood Artist reads 'this creature or another creature dies' with no controller restriction, so an opponent's creature counts). Both triggers are yours and you order them. Resolve: Insect token D is created; DRAIN 2 — opponent B loses 1 life (40 -> 39), you gain 1 (40 -> 41). Your Insects: B, C, D. Mana left: one untapped Swamp. Hand: 0. Library: 59. The point of this activation is that its cost is mana and a card, NOT a creature: it removes the Bears and still makes an Insect without spending a body.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.3b — “If multiple abilities have triggered since the last time a player received priority, the abilities are placed on the stack in a two-part process.”
  6. ACTIVATION 2 of the sacrifice ability: pay 1 life (41 -> 40), sacrifice Insect B, target Insect C. (Targeting the very Insect you are sacrificing would be legal to announce, but at resolution that target is no longer where it was when targeted, so the ability would not resolve at all — which is why this mode needs two Insects on the battlefield.)
    Blood Artist triggers on Insect B's death and goes on the stack above Yawgmoth's ability; it resolves first — DRAIN 3, opponent C 40 -> 39, you 40 -> 41. Yawgmoth's ability then resolves: -1/-1 counter on Insect C (1/1 -> 0/0), draw a card (library 58, hand 1). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Insect C for toughness 0, and Blood Artist triggers again — DRAIN 4, opponent D 40 -> 39, you 41 -> 42. Nest's trigger resolves: Insect token E. Net for the activation: two drains, one card drawn, and your Insect count fell from 3 to 2 (D and E).
    CR 608.2b — “A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
  7. ACTIVATION 3: pay 1 life (42 -> 41), sacrifice Insect D, target Insect E. Same shape as activation 2.
    DRAIN 5 (Insect D dies): opponent A 39 -> 38, you 41 -> 42. The counter makes Insect E 0/0 and state-based actions bury it; DRAIN 6: opponent B 39 -> 38, you 42 -> 43. Draw (library 57, hand 2). Nest makes Insect token F. Your Insect count: 1.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  8. ACTIVATION 4 in this line — the one that consumes the payoff. With a single Insect left there is no second Insect to target, and Yawgmoth cannot be targeted by his own ability: he is a Human Cleric permanent, so his ability is an ability from a source with the stated quality and his protection from Humans forbids it. The only remaining legal creature target is Blood Artist itself. Pay 1 life (43 -> 42), sacrifice Insect F, target Blood Artist.
    DRAIN 7 (Insect F dies): opponent C 39 -> 38, you 42 -> 43. Yawgmoth's ability resolves: a -1/-1 counter makes Blood Artist -1/0. Draw (library 56, hand 3). Nest triggers. State-based actions bury Blood Artist for toughness 0. Blood Artist's ability is a leaves-the-battlefield ability, and leaves-the-battlefield abilities are among the zone-change triggers that LOOK BACK IN TIME. The mechanism is the general rule those exceptions hang off: for such an ability the game looks back in time to determine if it triggers, using the existence of those abilities and the appearance of objects immediately prior to the event. So Blood Artist sees its own death: DRAIN 8, opponent D 39 -> 38, you 43 -> 44. Nest's trigger resolves into Insect token G. Blood Artist is now in the graveyard and NO further death in this line drains anyone.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.6c — “Leaves-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent moves from the battlefield to another zone, or when a phased-in permanent leaves the game because its owner leaves the game.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities”
    CR 603.10 — “the game “looks back in time” to determine if those abilities trigger, using the existence of those abilities and the appearance of objects immediately prior to the event”
  9. ANSWER (a), part 1 — separate what the BOARD fixes from what the LINE chooses.
    BOARD-LEVEL INVARIANT (the answer to 'how far does this board go'): a maximum of EIGHT creature deaths occur while Blood Artist is on the battlefield, hence EIGHT Blood Artist triggers and EIGHT life removed. That number is a property of the board and every optimal line reaches it. THE ACTIVATION COUNT IS LINE-DEPENDENT and is not a board property. Line 1, the one resolved in steps 1-8: four draining sacrifice activations plus one proliferate, after which a fifth sacrifice activation (sacrifice Insect G, zero targets — 'up to one target creature' permits zero, so the ability is not targeted at all) is legal but drains nobody because Blood Artist is already dead; that leaves Yawgmoth as your only creature and 'Sacrifice another creature' can never be paid again. Line 2, equally optimal: at step 8 instead sacrifice Insect F choosing ZERO targets (DRAIN 7; Blood Artist survives, no counter and so no Nest token), then as a fifth activation sacrifice BLOOD ARTIST itself with zero targets — its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees its death, DRAIN 8. That line runs FIVE draining sacrifice activations for the same eight drains, and ends at 43 life, library 55, with no Insect token G. So: 8 drains is the board's number; '4 draining activations' belongs to line 1 and '5' to line 2. ALTERNATIVES CHECKED AND REJECTED. (i) Skipping proliferate yields SEVEN drains, and the reason previously given — that killing the Bears with an activation 'costs a body' — is WITHDRAWN as false: that activation is body-NEUTRAL for you (sacrifice one Insect, Nest returns one, and the Bears was never yours). The true reason is that proliferate removes the Bears while spending no creature at all, so you enter the rest of the line one body richer, and on this board the remaining reach from any state is (drains so far) + 2 x (your Insects), because each Insect converts into its own death plus the death of whatever your counter kills. Proliferate branch: 2 + 2x3 = 8. Activation branch: 3 + 2x2 = 7. The extra body is worth 2 and the forgone activation only bought 1. (ii) Zero-target activations taken mid-line yield 1 drain instead of 2 for the same body, so they never beat targeting an Insect while a second Insect exists. (iii) Targeting the very creature you are sacrificing: the sacrifice puts it into the graveyard, so by the time the ability resolves it is no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted and the only target is illegal, so the ability does not resolve (and being a token it has by then ceased to exist as well — 704.5d, cited at step 1 — which is stronger than the illegality needs), costing you the counter, the Nest token AND the card draw, for one drain instead of two.
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen. Such a spell or ability is still said to require targets, but that spell or ability is targeted only if one or more targets have been chosen for it.”
    CR 608.2b — “If all its targets, for every instance of the word “target,” are now illegal, the spell or ability doesn’t resolve.”
  10. ANSWER (a), part 2 — what ends it. Test each candidate bound named in the question.
    NOT your library: line 1 draws 4 cards and 56 of 60 remain (line 2 draws 5 and leaves 55), so the deck-out state-based action is never approached. NOT your life: you pay 1 life per sacrifice activation and gain 1 life per drain, 4 paid against 8 gained, so you finish at 44 — HIGHER than you started. Life is a net gain and can never be the constraint while Blood Artist lives. NOT opposing toughness as a grind: Grizzly Bears' toughness of 2 supplies exactly ONE survivable counter, and that window closes in step 3. What ends the engine is RUNNING OUT OF BODIES AND LEGAL TARGETS, by arithmetic: once Grizzly Bears is dead, every remaining creature target (a 1/1 Insect, the 0/1 Blood Artist) dies to a single counter, so such an activation removes two of your creatures while Nest returns one — net minus one. (Separately and truly: state-based actions bury the target before Nest's trigger is put on the stack, which is why the replacement Insect always arrives after the death it replaces and can never be that activation's target. That is a targeting fact, not the source of the minus one.) Yawgmoth's protection from Humans forbids him as a target and 'another creature' forbids him as the sacrifice, so the last legal target is Blood Artist and the engine's final act is eating its own payoff. This is a strictly finite chain, not a loop with no way to stop, so no draw question arises.
    CR 704.5b — “If a player attempted to draw a card from a library with no cards in it since the last time state-based actions were checked, that player loses the game.”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
  11. ANSWER (b) — total reach across the pod, and the straight yes/no.
    EIGHT life total is removed from the opposing side, out of 160. Blood Artist's ability says 'target player loses 1 life' — one instance of the word target, singular — and a triggered ability's targets are declared when it is put on the stack, by the same announcement process a spell uses. Each of the eight triggers is therefore a SEPARATE ability object making its own single choice, so the eight may be spread or all aimed at one player, but no single trigger can ever reach more than one. Eight creature deaths occurred while Blood Artist was on the battlefield (Insect A, Grizzly Bears, Insect B, Insect C, Insect D, Insect E, Insect F, Blood Artist itself), so eight triggers and eight points of life loss. Spread evenly the pilot takes 2 from each opponent: 38 / 38 / 38 / 38. Concentrated on one player it takes that player from 40 to 32 and leaves the other three at 40. NO — this line cannot reduce all four opponents from 40 to 0. It falls short by 152, and the requirement is not a few more iterations but 160 creature deaths with Blood Artist on the battlefield against the 8 this board produces. The single-target wording is doing a fraction of the work of 'each opponent loses 1 life', but the substitute figure is NOT one number, and the reason is BODIES rather than the Bears. This board holds three creature bodies: Blood Artist, Insect A (already sacrificed to pay the cost) and Insect B. Both substitutes read 'creature you control' — Zulaport Cutthroat 'this creature or another creature you control dies', Bastion of Remembrance 'a creature you control dies' — so both miss Grizzly Bears, opponent A's creature, identically; that is the same one death for each and it is not what separates them. ZULAPORT CUTTHROAT IS ITSELF A CREATURE. Substituted into Blood Artist's slot it preserves all three bodies, so the terminal activation still has a legal creature target (Zulaport itself), Zulaport dies as the engine's final act and its own leaves-the-battlefield trigger sees that death: SEVEN deaths of creatures you control, the seventh being the drainer itself, and 7 x 4 = 28 life removed. Penalty against Blood Artist's 8: 3.5x. BASTION OF REMEMBRANCE IS AN ENCHANTMENT AND NEVER DIES. Substituted into that same slot it REMOVES a body and leaves two, so the terminal activation has no legal creature target at all — Yawgmoth is unreachable through protection from Humans — it takes zero targets, Bastion is never on the receiving end of anything, and only Insects A through F die: SIX deaths of creatures you control, and 6 x 4 = 24 life removed. Penalty: 3.0x. SISTER-STACK RECONCILIATION, stated LIKE-FOR-LIKE so this does not read as self-contradiction. Both substitution figures above are computed on a WITH-proliferate line (the proliferate of step 5), so each may only be set against a with-proliferate figure. Stack 003's board is identical to this one but for the drainer slot and it also runs the proliferate, so its 28 for Zulaport IS the 28 reached here — like-for-like, and it stands. Stack 002's headline 28 is a DIFFERENT QUANTITY: it is 002's NO-proliferate board invariant, and 002 carries a separate proliferate_variant reporting 8 deaths of creatures you control, 32 life removed, each opponent at 32 and you at 43, with the explicit instruction that the two are never blended. So the like-for-like Bastion comparison is this board's 24 against 002's 32, NOT against 002's 28. The whole of that 8-life gap is ONE BODY: 002's board carries a 1/1 white Human Soldier token — the very token Bastion's own enters-the-battlefield ability creates — which this board does not, so 002 runs its with-proliferate line from three creature bodies (Insect A, Insect B, the Soldier) where this board has two (Insect A, Insect B). Under step 9's own rule an extra body on this engine is worth TWO deaths, its own plus the death of whatever its counter kills, so the delta is +2 deaths and +2 x 4 = +8 life: 6 deaths / 24 life here, 8 deaths / 32 life there. The +2 holds exactly even though a Bastion line's TERMINAL body yields only one death (with no creature drainer to receive the last counter, the final activation takes zero targets), because both boards pay that same terminal penalty once. It is a two-death delta, not the one-death 'restored seventh death' asserted in the previous iteration, which is withdrawn as false. Neither figure is a correction to 002. Without the proliferate the Blood Artist figure is 7, distributed 2 / 2 / 2 / 1, and the answer is the same NO.
    CR 603.3d — “The remainder of the process for putting a triggered ability on the stack is identical to the process for casting a spell listed in rules 601.2c–d.”
    CR 115.1 — “These targets are declared as part of the process of putting the spell or ability on the stack.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”

A-005 Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs + South Wind Avatar ✓ cleared, 4 cycle(s) · 12 steps · 32 citations
Rules version June 19, 2026
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) South Wind Avatar reads 'Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness' and 'Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life'. A dying 1/1 Insect has toughness 1 at the moment it dies only if it carries no -1/-1 counter — state what its toughness actually is when it dies here, and therefore whether the lifegain trigger fires at all. Give the exact number of completed activations and what ends the loop. (b) South Wind Avatar is a 3/4 — the only body on this board that can absorb more than one -1/-1 counter. Does that change the bound, and by how much?
  1. Account for the cost that has already been paid. The scenario's board list annotates Insect token A as "already sacrificed to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack", so A is NOT on the battlefield as this resolution begins: Yawgmoth's ability was activated by paying 1 life (you 40 -> 39) and sacrificing Insect token A. Sacrificing A moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard, so A died, and South Wind Avatar's first ability ('Whenever another creature you control dies, you gain life equal to its toughness') triggered on that cost payment. Stated explicitly so nothing is left to inference: every number below is computed from that reading of the board, which is the same house convention this deck's stack 001 uses for a sacrifice paid as a cost.
    The Avatar's death trigger is waiting to be put on the stack. It goes on the stack the next time a player would receive priority - which is immediately after activation finishes - so it sits ABOVE Yawgmoth's activated ability and will resolve FIRST. The scenario's stack listing (one object) is therefore incomplete: the true stack is [pos 0: Yawgmoth's ability, pos 1: Avatar lifegain trigger].
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means “is put into a graveyard from the battlefield.””
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  2. Fix the targets and the permanent targeting constraint. Yawgmoth's ability reads 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card.' Its target (Grizzly Bears) was locked in as the ability was activated, not on resolution - and 115.1c points at 602.2b, which makes the whole 601.2b-i announcement sequence apply to activating an ability. Yawgmoth himself has protection from Humans and is the source of a Human's ability, so he is never a legal target for it.
    Grizzly Bears is the target for this activation. For every later activation, the legal target pool is: Grizzly Bears (while alive), South Wind Avatar (while alive), and Insect tokens on the battlefield at the moment targets are announced - never Yawgmoth. Nest of Scarabs is an enchantment, not a creature, so it is never a target either. The supply of creatures that can absorb a counter without costing you a body is what ultimately ends the engine.
    CR 115.1c — “The target(s) are chosen as the ability is activated; see rule 602.2b.”
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
  3. Resolve the top object: South Wind Avatar's lifegain trigger for Insect token A. This is a leaves-the-battlefield/dies trigger, one of the trigger classes that looks back in time, and 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so the value comes from last known information. Insect A carried NO -1/-1 counter - it was sacrificed as a cost, not killed by a counter - so its last known toughness on the battlefield was 1.
    You gain 1 life. Life: you 39 -> 40.
    CR 603.6c — “Leaves-the-battlefield abilities trigger when a permanent moves from the battlefield to another zone, or when a phased-in permanent leaves the game because its owner leaves the game.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities, abilities that trigger when a player sacrifices a permanent, abilities that trigger when a card leaves a graveyard, and abilities that trigger when an object that all players can see is put into a hand or library.”
    CR 608.2h — “If the effect requires information from a specific object, including the source of the ability itself, the effect uses the current information of that object if it’s in the public zone it was expected to be in; if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. The life gain is a real (nonzero) life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's second ability ('Whenever you gain life, each opponent loses 1 life') triggers. Cited here rather than assumed: an ability written 'whenever you gain life' is treated as though it read 'whenever a source causes you to gain life', so it keys on the EVENT and fires once per gain, whatever the size of the gain. It goes on the stack and resolves.
    Each of the four opponents loses 1 life. Opponents: 40 -> 39 each. Four life lost in total from this one drain - 1 per opponent per lifegain event, never 1 per point of life gained.
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 119.9 — “Some triggered abilities are written, “Whenever [a player] gains life, . . . .” Such abilities are treated as though they are written, “Whenever a source causes [a player] to gain life, . . . .””
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  5. Resolve Yawgmoth's activated ability: put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness, making Grizzly Bears 1/1. Nest of Scarabs reads 'Whenever YOU put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens', and that condition has two halves, both of which must be shown. (i) A counter is put on a creature: Grizzly Bears is a creature, and the trigger is unrestricted as to whose creature it is, so an opponent's creature counts. (ii) YOU put it: the counter is placed by a resolving activated ability you control, and it is the controller of an ability who follows its instructions, so you are the player putting the counter. Both halves are met, so Nest triggers (trigger matching per CR 603.2, cited at step 4). Then resolve that Nest of Scarabs trigger, the next object on the stack: create one 1/1 black Insect creature token (call it C).
    Grizzly Bears is 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter. You draw a card (library 60 -> 59). Nest of Scarabs triggered once, for one counter, and that trigger resolves into Insect C. ACTIVATION 1 COMPLETE: you control Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs, South Wind Avatar (3/4), Insect B and Insect C. Body count is NET NEUTRAL - one Insect was consumed as a cost and one was created by the counter - and life is net neutral too (paid 1, gained 1). This is why the engine is a loop rather than a one-shot, and why neither life nor the library is what bounds it. The bound is the supply of -1/-1 counter targets that can absorb a counter without costing you a body.
    CR 122.1a — “A +X/+Y counter on a creature or on a creature card in a zone other than the battlefield, where X and Y are numbers, adds X to that object’s power and Y to that object’s toughness. Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 111.1 — “Some effects put tokens onto the battlefield. A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn’t represented by a card.”
  6. ACTIVATION 2. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect B - it has no counters, so its last known toughness is 1: the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40), which drains each opponent for 1 (opponents 38 each). The ability resolves putting the second -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears, making it 0/0. Before any player gets priority, state-based actions run first and put it in the graveyard; only then do triggered abilities go on the stack.
    Grizzly Bears dies. It is an OPPONENT'S creature, so South Wind Avatar's 'another creature YOU CONTROL dies' ability does not trigger for it - no life, no drain from its death. The Nest of Scarabs trigger from the counter still happened at the moment the counter was placed and is unaffected by the subsequent death: Insect D is created. Draw (library 58). Insects: C, D. Grizzly Bears has now absorbed its full 2 toughness worth of counters - 2 activations' worth of replacement Insects.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
  7. THE CRUX - what an Insect's toughness actually is when a -1/-1 counter kills it. ACTIVATIONS 3, 4 and 5 each sacrifice a counterless Insect (the Avatar gains you 1, each opponent loses 1) and put counters 1, 2 and 3 on South Wind Avatar - a 3/4, and -1/-1 counters subtract from POWER as well as toughness (122.1a, cited at step 5), so it goes 2/3, then 1/2, then 0/1, never dying, because a 4-toughness body absorbs 3 counters and is still alive at 1 toughness - each counter making one replacement Insect. ACTIVATION 6 has THREE legal targets at announcement - South Wind Avatar, still alive at 0/1 with three counters, and BOTH Insects then on the battlefield, F and G. F counts precisely because targets are announced at 601.2c before the cost is paid at 601.2h (step 9): F is still on the battlefield when targets are chosen, so it is a legal target, even though paying the cost is about to sacrifice it. Targeting F is NOT self-defeating, and the reason step 9 gives for declining to target Insect J does not carry here. Step 9's argument works only because J is at that point the ONLY sacrificeable body, so targeting it forces the cost to sacrifice the very permanent it targeted. At activation 6 there are two counterless Insects, and the sacrifice is chosen at 601.2h - AFTER targets are announced at 601.2c (step 9) - so targeting F simply means you pay the cost with G instead. F and G are interchangeable counterless 1/1 tokens, so the two options are EQUIVALENT by F-G symmetry, not one good and one self-defeating. Activation 6's real choice is therefore binary: spend the counter on an Insect (F or G, identically) or on the Avatar, and it is decided on other grounds - the Avatar line is already costed below. The best line spends the counter on one of your own Insects, say G, sacrificing F. It is not compelled to. (The alternative is legal and instructive: spending the Avatar's fourth counter here still reaches 8 activations, but it retires the drain two activations early and yields only 6 drains, 24 opposing life instead of 28. That choice is where 4 points of drain live.) The targeted Insect becomes 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by the same state-based action that killed Grizzly Bears at step 6 (CR 704.5f), and - being a token - it then ceases to exist. The Avatar's first ability DOES trigger on that death and DOES resolve; but 'its toughness' is information about an object no longer on the battlefield, so it is read from last known information, and the Insect's last known toughness ON the battlefield was 0, not 1, because the counter was already on it. TRIGGER ORDERING, since two of your triggered abilities are waiting at that same moment: Nest of Scarabs (triggered mid-resolution, when the counter was placed) and the Avatar's dies-trigger (triggered during the state-based action check that killed the Insect). Neither has 'another ability triggering' as its trigger condition, so both go on the stack together in the first part of the 603.3b process, in whichever order you choose. The order is outcome-neutral here: the Nest trigger creates its token either way, and the Avatar's trigger gains 0 either way.
    You gain 0 life from that death. Gaining 0 life is not a life gain event, so South Wind Avatar's SECOND ability does not trigger at all: no opponent loses life for that Insect. Stated plainly - a counter-killed Insect is worth 0 life and 0 drain; only a SACRIFICED (counterless) Insect, last known toughness 1, is worth 1 life and 4 opposing life. Exactly one death in the optimal line is of the counter-killed kind. Running totals after activation 6: opponents 34 each, you 40, library 54, Insects: one (H). South Wind Avatar is 0/1 with three counters (a 3/4 reduced by three -1/-1 counters, power and toughness alike).
    CR 111.7 — “A token that’s in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist.”
    CR 608.2h — “If the effect requires information from a specific object, including the source of the ability itself, the effect uses the current information of that object if it’s in the public zone it was expected to be in; if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information.”
    CR 119.9 — “If a player gains 0 life, no life gain event has occurred, and these abilities won’t trigger.”
    CR 603.3b — “First, each player, in APNAP order, puts each triggered ability they control with a trigger condition that isn’t another ability triggering on the stack in any order they choose.”
  8. ACTIVATION 7 - the Avatar's fourth toughness point, and its own death. Pay 1 life (you 39), sacrifice Insect H (counterless, toughness 1): the Avatar gains you 1 life (you 40) and each opponent loses 1 (opponents 33 each). Critically, the Avatar is still on the battlefield when that sacrifice happens - the cost is paid before the ability resolves - so this drain is collected in full. The ability then resolves, putting the FOURTH -1/-1 counter on South Wind Avatar, making it -1/0. State-based actions put it into the graveyard.
    South Wind Avatar dies. Its own ability reads 'Whenever ANOTHER creature you control dies', so its own death does not match its trigger event and it does not trigger for itself - no final life, no final drain. This is a permanent fact about the Avatar and it is load-bearing for the ceiling proved at step 11: the Avatar can never be its own drain. The Nest of Scarabs trigger from that fourth counter still resolves and makes Insect J - a body that is created AFTER the Avatar has already been put into the graveyard. Draw (library 53). The lifegain/drain engine is now off permanently: every later sacrifice gains nothing and drains nothing.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  9. ACTIVATION 8 and the terminus, narrated in the order the rules actually run. Activating an ability follows the 601.2 sequence (step 2's 115.1c points at 602.2b for exactly this), and in that sequence targets are ANNOUNCED at 601.2c and the total cost is paid five lettered steps later at 601.2h, with 601.2d (dividing or distributing an effect), 601.2e (the legality check), 601.2f (determining the total cost) and 601.2g (activating mana abilities) all intervening. The only thing the argument needs is the DIRECTION - announcement precedes payment - and that direction is what 601.2c and 601.2h jointly fix. So at the moment targets are chosen for activation 8, Insect J is still on the battlefield and IS a legal target; Yawgmoth is not (step 2). Choosing zero targets is therefore a CHOICE the ability permits ('up to one target creature'), not a compulsion forced by an empty target pool. It is the correct choice: had J been targeted, paying the cost would then have sacrificed J, moving it off the battlefield (step 1's 701.21a), and on resolution the ability's only target would have been illegal - so the ability would not have resolved at all and you would have lost the card. With no target chosen, the cost is paid: 1 life (you 39) and Insect J sacrificed. No Avatar is on the battlefield, so no lifegain trigger and no drain. The ability resolves; no counter is placed, so Nest of Scarabs does not trigger and no replacement Insect is created; you draw a card (library 53 -> 52).
    LOOP ENDS. You now control only Yawgmoth, Nest of Scarabs and three Swamps - no other creature. The activation cost is 'Pay 1 life, Sacrifice another creature'; with no other creature on the battlefield, that cost is unpayable and cannot be paid, so there is no ninth activation. Note what the terminus rests on: the SACRIFICE COST becoming unpayable, not the target pool being empty. The engine is bounded by BODIES - not by life, which was net zero for as long as the Avatar lived and then cost 1 per activation out of a 40 life total.
    CR 601.2c — “The player announces their choice of an appropriate object or player for each target the spell requires.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal. A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
    CR 608.2b — “If all its targets, for every instance of the word “target,” are now illegal, the spell or ability doesn’t resolve.”
    CR 601.2h — “Partial payments are not allowed. Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
  10. DISPOSE OF PROLIFERATE - Yawgmoth's OTHER activated ability, which the scenario's mana line invites. Yawgmoth, Thran Physician also reads '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate.' The scenario grants {B}{B}{B} from three untapped Swamps, which the first ability never spends, and the hand starts empty but holds a card from activation 1's draw onward - so exactly ONE proliferate is available in this sequence (three Swamps cannot make a second {B}{B}). To proliferate is to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter and give each one more counter of each kind it already has. What that can reach here: Grizzly Bears while it carries one counter, and South Wind Avatar once it carries counters. It can NEVER reach one of your Insects - a 1/1 Insect that takes a -1/-1 counter is 0/0 and is put into the graveyard by a state-based action before any player receives priority to activate anything (step 6's 704.5f), so no Insect ever bears a counter at a time when proliferate could be activated. No player here has poison or any other counter.
    It does NOT change either number. A proliferated -1/-1 counter is a counter YOU put on a creature, so Nest of Scarabs triggers and makes a token for free - no sacrifice, no life paid (step 5). But the token comes out of the SAME fixed pool the ceiling is computed from: the only creatures that can absorb a counter without costing you a body are Grizzly Bears and the Avatar, and their printed toughness caps that pool at 2 + 4 = 6 counters no matter who places them. A counter proliferate places is a counter no later activation can place, so it buys a body 1-for-1 out of capacity already counted at step 11. Activations stay at 8. Drains stay at 7 as well, because proliferate produces no death of a creature YOU control that gains nonzero life: it cannot kill an Insect (it can never target one), Grizzly Bears is an opponent's, and if it is used to finish the Avatar, the Avatar's death never triggers the Avatar's own ability (step 8). So: {B}{B}{B} on the board is real and the ability is genuinely activatable, and the answer to (a) and (b) is unchanged at 8 activations and 7 drains. (Sister stack 002, where proliferate DOES move the drain count from 7 to 8, differs precisely because that board's drain fires on your creatures dying at any toughness; here a counter-killed body gains 0 and drains nothing.)
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
  11. PROVE THE BOUND, by case exhaustion over how the Avatar leaves the battlefield - and answer (b). (i) THE BODY LEDGER. Every completed activation consumes exactly one creature other than Yawgmoth (the sacrifice) and returns exactly one Insect for each -1/-1 counter placed on a creature. A counter placed on one of YOUR Insects is body-neutral: +1 token, -1 Insect (step 7). A counter placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar adds a body without removing one of yours - except the counter that finally kills the Avatar, which removes the Avatar itself as a sacrificeable body. Absorption is capped by printed toughness: Bears 2, Avatar 4. So the total number of creatures you can ever sacrifice is 3 (Insect A, Insect B, the Avatar) + (counters on Bears, at most 2) + (counters on the Avatar, at most 4) - (1 if the Avatar's counters killed it). Enumerate the cases: [A] the Avatar takes 4 counters and dies to them - 3 + 2 + 4 - 1 = 8; [B] the Avatar takes at most 3 counters and is SACRIFICED instead - 3 + 2 + 3 - 0 = 8; [C] the Avatar neither dies to a counter nor is sacrificed - it is then not a sacrificeable body at all, giving 2 + 2 + 3 = 7. No case exceeds 8, and steps 1-9 attain 8, so the bound is tight at 8. (This replaces the earlier claim that an eighth drain 'would require the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice, which caps activations at 7' - that reasoning is false, and case [B] is the counterexample: leave the Avatar on three counters and sacrifice it as activation 8 and you have 8 activations with the Avatar alive at the eighth sacrifice.) (ii) THE DRAIN CEILING IS 7, and the correct reason has two parts. A drain requires a nonzero lifegain event, which requires a creature you control to die with last known toughness at least 1 while the Avatar is on the battlefield; the only deaths that qualify are sacrifices of counterless Insects, because a counter-killed Insect dies at toughness 0 (step 7), Grizzly Bears is not yours (step 6), and the Avatar's own death never triggers its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). So drains are at most one per sacrifice, at most 8. That 8 is unreachable, and the proof is by SUPPLY OF BODIES - not by any claim that an 8-activation line sacrifices everything it has, which is FALSE: Insect G is consumed by a counter-kill and never sacrificed at all, which is the whole content of step 7. Suppose instead, for contradiction, that all 8 sacrifices drained. Each would then have to be the sacrifice of a counterless INSECT made while the Avatar was still on the battlefield - the Avatar itself can never be one of them, since its own death does not trigger its own 'another creature you control dies' ability (step 8). Now count how many Insect bodies can exist under that supposition. You start with 2 (A and B). Every further Insect is a Nest token, one per -1/-1 counter placed, and a counter placed on one of your own Insects is exactly body-neutral - it kills one and creates one (step 7) - so only counters placed on Grizzly Bears or on the Avatar can grow the supply. Bears absorbs at most 2. The Avatar can have absorbed at most 3 while still being on the battlefield, because a fourth counter kills it (step 8) and the supposition requires it alive at every one of the 8 sacrifices. That is at most 2 + 3 = 5 supply-growing counters, hence at most 5 tokens, hence at most 2 + 5 = 7 Insect bodies exist in time to be sacrificed - one short of the 8 the supposition demands. Contradiction, so at most 7 drains. The count is unchanged if proliferate places some of those counters (step 10), since they come out of the same fixed 2 + 4 absorption pool. The case split says the same thing concretely: in case [B] the Avatar leaves by being sacrificed and that sacrifice is its own death, which does not trigger it; in case [A] it is counter-killed, and the Nest token made by the killing counter is created after the Avatar is already in the graveyard (step 8) - a body that, being a token from a counter-kill rather than the last body standing, still has to be disposed of with no Avatar on the battlefield. Either way at least one of the 8 sacrifices is drainless, which is what the supply count proves in general - and the line in steps 1-9 attains 7, simultaneously with 8 activations.
    (b) YES, the 4-toughness body extends the bound, by exactly +4 activations. South Wind Avatar contributes 4 of the 6 non-Insect counter slots (+4), adds itself as a sacrificeable body (+1), and loses itself as a body to the very counter that kills it (-1): net +4. Without it, the ledger reads 2 starting Insects + 2 Grizzly Bears counters = 4 activations, and ZERO drains, since it is also the only source of lifegain on the board - so it supplies 100% of the damage. The library is not the constraint either: 8 draws against a stated 60-card library, where the rule that punishes an empty library would need roughly 60 activations against a hard ceiling of 8.
    CR 601.2h — “Partial payments are not allowed. Unpayable costs can’t be paid.”
    CR 104.3c — “If a player is required to draw more cards than are left in their library, they draw the remaining cards and then lose the game the next time a player would receive priority.”
  12. Tally. South Wind Avatar's first ability triggered 8 times over the run: seven sacrifices of counterless 1/1 Insects (last known toughness 1, so 1 life each) plus one counter-killed Insect at toughness 0 (0 life). Its second ability triggered 7 times, once per nonzero lifegain event, draining 1 from each of four opponents each time.
    (a) 8 completed activations, ended by having no creature other than Yawgmoth left to sacrifice. The Avatar's drain DOES fire - 7 times, not 8 and not 10 - for 7 x 4 = 28 total opposing life. Each opponent goes 40 -> 33; 160 opposing life becomes 132. You end at 39 (40 - 8 paid + 7 gained), having drawn 8 cards (library 52). This is decisively NOT infinite and not near-infinite: the deaths that would have to be free are the counter-killed Insects, and those gain 0 life and drain nothing. Commander Spellbook's 'Near-infinite' tag is wrong for this board.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
After the line
Your life
39
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, no counters, untapped), Nest of Scarabs, Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped)
Opponents
Opponent A: 33, Opponent B: 33, Opponent C: 33, Opponent D: 33

A-006 Persist + Nest of Scarabs ✓ cleared, 2 cycle(s) · 6 steps · 21 citations
Rules version June 19, 2026
The question put to the record
Persist resolves and returns Fume Spitter to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter on it. Does Nest of Scarabs trigger and create an Insect token? Cite the rule that decides whether a permanent entering the battlefield with counters counts as a player putting those counters on it, and state explicitly whether the answer would differ if an effect instead placed a counter on a creature already on the battlefield.
  1. Persist resolves. Its one target is rechecked for legality first: Fume Spitter is still a nonlegendary creature card in your graveyard, so the target is legal and the spell resolves. You follow its instruction in the order written: return Fume Spitter from your graveyard to the battlefield with a -1/-1 counter on it. Persist does not name a controller, so Fume Spitter enters under YOUR control (you are the player the effect instructed to put it onto the battlefield). Persist itself is then put into your graveyard as the final part of its resolution.
    Fume Spitter is on the battlefield under your control with one -1/-1 counter on it. Persist is in your graveyard. Nothing has been targeted or damaged; life totals are unchanged at 40 apiece.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 110.2a — “If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player’s control unless the effect states otherwise.”
    CR 608.2n — “As the final part of an instant or sorcery spell’s resolution, the spell is put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  2. THE RULING, and it is decided by one rule: 122.6. Nest of Scarabs asks 'Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature.' Rule 122.6 says that a spell or ability referring to counters being put on an object refers BOTH to putting counters on it while it is on the battlefield AND to an object that is given counters as it enters the battlefield. So 'entering with a -1/-1 counter' is not a separate, exempt category — it is inside the reference. The second half of the question, WHO put it, is answered by 122.6a: the effect may specify a player, and Persist does not, so the object's controller puts those counters on it. Fume Spitter's controller is you (step 1). Therefore YOU put one -1/-1 counter on a creature, and Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition is satisfied. ANSWER: YES, Nest of Scarabs triggers.
    The trigger condition of Nest of Scarabs is met. Note the citation this step deliberately does NOT lean on: sibling stacks 001-005 reach 'you are the actor' through 608.2c, because there the counter is placed by an ability you control following its instructions on a creature already on the battlefield. That reasoning does not by itself reach a permanent entering with counters — no instruction is being followed against an object on the battlefield at that moment. It is 122.6 that brings the entering case inside the phrase, and 122.6a that names you rather than 608.2c. Both roads arrive at the same place here, but only 122.6/122.6a is load-bearing for THIS scenario.
    CR 122.6 — “This refers to putting counters on that object while it’s on the battlefield and also to an object that’s given counters as it enters the battlefield.”
    CR 122.6a — “the effect causing the object to be given counters may specify which player puts those counters on it”
    CR 122.6a — “If the effect doesn’t specify a player, the object’s controller puts those counters on it.”
    CR 110.2a — “If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player’s control unless the effect states otherwise.”
  3. The trigger fires. The game event (you putting one -1/-1 counter on a creature) matches Nest of Scarabs' trigger condition, so the ability triggers automatically at that moment. It does nothing yet and is not yet on the stack.
    One Nest of Scarabs trigger is waiting to be put on the stack. 'That many' is locked to the number of -1/-1 counters in the event: ONE.
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers. The ability doesn’t do anything at this point.”
  4. State-based actions are checked BEFORE the waiting trigger goes on the stack. Fume Spitter is a 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter on it, which subtracts from power and toughness, making it 0/0. A creature with toughness 0 or less is put into its owner's graveyard — Fume Spitter dies immediately, without ever having been available to sacrifice. The check is then repeated; nothing more is performed, and the Nest of Scarabs trigger is put on the stack. Fume Spitter dying does NOT undo the trigger: it had already triggered in step 3, and the trigger is an independent object on the stack that no longer depends on its source event or on Fume Spitter.
    Fume Spitter is in your graveyard. Your battlefield: Nest of Scarabs, three untapped Swamps. Stack: one Nest of Scarabs trigger. This is the pilot's real lesson — Persist on a 1-toughness creature returns it and kills it in the same breath, and what you actually keep is the Insect.
    CR 122.1a — “Similarly, -X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event (see rule 704, “State-Based Actions”), then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack (see rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities”).”
    CR 704.3 — “If any state-based actions are performed as a result of a check, the check is repeated; otherwise all triggered abilities that are waiting to be put on the stack are put on the stack, then the check is repeated.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
  5. You receive priority and pass; every opponent passes in succession (the scenario's stated assumption), so the Nest of Scarabs trigger resolves. It creates that many — one — 1/1 black Insect creature tokens: put one token with those characteristics onto the battlefield under your control. The ability then leaves the stack.
    One 1/1 black Insect creature token enters the battlefield under your control. State-based actions are checked again and none apply (the Insect is 1/1, nothing else is on the battlefield to check). You receive priority in your precombat main phase with the stack empty.
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 701.7a — “To create one or more tokens with certain characteristics, put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield.”
    CR 111.1 — “Some effects put tokens onto the battlefield. A token is a marker used to represent any permanent that isn’t represented by a card.”
    CR 608.2n — “As the final part of an ability’s resolution, the ability is removed from the stack and ceases to exist.”
  6. THE CONTRAST THE QUESTION ASKS FOR: would the answer differ if an effect instead placed a -1/-1 counter on a creature ALREADY on the battlefield? No. The answer is YES in both cases, and 122.6 is precisely the rule that makes them the same — it names both halves in one sentence. What changes is only which rule identifies YOU as the actor. For a creature already on the battlefield, an ability you control instructs you to put the counter and you follow that instruction (608.2c) — that is the route stacks 001-005 take. For a permanent entering with counters, no instruction is being followed against an on-battlefield object, so the actor comes from 122.6a's default: the object's controller. THE ONE PLACE THE TWO GENUINELY DIVERGE, and a pilot should know it: because 122.6a hands the counters to the object's CONTROLLER, a nonlegendary creature returned to the battlefield under an OPPONENT's control with a -1/-1 counter would have that counter put on by that opponent, not by you, and Nest of Scarabs — which says 'whenever YOU put' — would not trigger. In this scenario Fume Spitter enters under your control, so that caveat does not bite.
    Ruling for the deck: Persist IS a legitimate ignition source for Nest of Scarabs — the -1/-1 counter it brings along counts as you putting a counter on a creature, and the Nest pays out one Insect. The goldfish_targets.json listing of Persist as an ignition source is NOT overstated on the rules. The honest caveat is a different one and it is about the BODY, not the trigger: Persist returning a 1-toughness creature (Fume Spitter) yields the Insect and immediately loses the creature to state-based actions, so it ignites without leaving a sacrifice body behind.
    CR 122.6 — “Some spells and abilities refer to counters being put on an object. This refers to putting counters on that object while it’s on the battlefield and also to an object that’s given counters as it enters the battlefield.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 122.6a — “If the effect doesn’t specify a player, the object’s controller puts those counters on it.”
After the line

40
Your battlefield
Nest of Scarabs (enchantment), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), Swamp (untapped), 1/1 black Insect creature token (created by Nest of Scarabs)
