A-008 Mirkwood Bats + Yawgmoth + Nest of Scarabs ✓ cleared, 5 cycle(s) · 11 steps · 32 citations
Rules version June 19, 2026
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then run the engine as far as the rules allow. (a) How many Mirkwood Bats triggers does ONE Yawgmoth activation paid with an Insect token produce, and why? (b) Over the whole engine, how many Bats triggers occur in total and how much life does each opponent lose — and state whether that is more than the 28 that stacks 002 and 003 measured on this same board with a death-metering drainer.
  1. Fix the reading and restore the trigger the stack picture omits. THE READING: the scenario's board annotates 'Insect token A' as ALREADY SACRIFICED to pay the cost of the ability now on the stack, so the pilot enters this resolution holding exactly one live Insect (token B) plus Mirkwood Bats (2/3) and Yawgmoth (2/4). THE OMISSION: sacrificing Insect A was a sacrifice of a token while Mirkwood Bats was on the battlefield, so Bats has already triggered and that trigger is not shown. Establish precisely when it went on the stack, because the whole question turns on the two halves of Bats' condition arriving at different times. Activating Yawgmoth's ability put the ability itself on the stack FIRST (602.2a) and the activation cost -- 1 life and 'Sacrifice another creature' -- was paid LAST, as the final step of the activation process (602.2b routing to 601.2h). So Insect A was sacrificed (701.21a) while Yawgmoth's ability was already the topmost object on the stack. Nothing happens at the moment an ability triggers; the Bats trigger waits and is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority (117.2a), which is immediately after the activation completes, and it becomes the topmost object (603.3) -- i.e. ABOVE Yawgmoth's ability.
    Stack (top-down): Mirkwood Bats' trigger from Insect A's sacrifice; Yawgmoth's -1/-1 ability targeting Grizzly Bears. You are at 39 (1 life already paid); each opponent at 40. Bats trigger count: 1. This is the sacrifice half of Bats' condition and it is already banked BEFORE Yawgmoth's ability has done anything at all.
    CR 602.2a — “It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 602.2b — “The remainder of the process for activating an ability is identical to the process for casting a spell”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 117.2a — “Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”
    CR 603.3 — “The ability becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
  2. Bats trigger #1 resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life.' Four opponents, four separate life losses. Bats grants YOU no life -- unlike the death-metering drainer of stack 003, which gained you 1 per drain.
    Opponents A/B/C/D: 40 -> 39 each. You remain at 39. Bats triggers so far: 1. Table life removed: 4.
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  3. Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Its target is rechecked first: Grizzly Bears is still on the battlefield and unchanged, so it is still legal (608.2b). Put a -1/-1 counter on Grizzly Bears and draw a card. The counter subtracts from power and toughness (122.1a), so Bears is 1/1.
    Grizzly Bears: 2/2 -> 1/1 with one -1/-1 counter, toughness above 0, survives. Library 60 -> 59, hand 1. Putting that counter triggers Nest of Scarabs ('Whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on a creature, create that many 1/1 black Insect creature tokens'). Mirkwood Bats does NOT trigger on the counter -- Bats meters creation and sacrifice of tokens, not counters and not deaths.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 122.1a — “-X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
  4. ANSWER (a). State-based actions find nothing (Bears is 1/1), the Nest of Scarabs trigger is put on the stack, all players pass in succession per the scenario's stated assumption (117.4), and it resolves: exactly ONE -1/-1 counter was placed, so Nest creates 'that many' = one 1/1 black Insect token (701.7a). Creating it is the OTHER half of Bats' condition, so Bats triggers a second time; that trigger then resolves for another 1 life from each opponent. On the singular wording: Bats reads 'a token', and 603.2c settles both directions -- an ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs, but repeatedly if ONE event contains multiple occurrences. So a Nest trigger that placed N counters at once and created N tokens in a single event would trigger Bats N times, once per token; every Nest trigger in this line places exactly one counter and creates exactly one token, so each is worth exactly one Bats trigger. THEREFORE: one Yawgmoth activation paid with an Insect token and placing a counter produces TWO Bats triggers -- one for the sacrifice, one for the creation -- and they are NOT simultaneous and never sit on the stack together. The sacrifice trigger was on the stack ABOVE the activated ability and resolved before it (step 1); the creation trigger did not exist until Nest's own trigger resolved, two resolutions later. An activation paid with a token that places no counter is worth exactly ONE (the sacrifice half only).
    Insect token C is created. Opponents 39 -> 38 each. Bats triggers so far: 2 -- and the first Yawgmoth activation is complete, so the answer to (a) is TWO. Your board: Yawgmoth 2/4, Nest of Scarabs, Mirkwood Bats 2/3, Insect B, Insect C. Bears 1/1. Table life removed: 8.
    CR 117.4 — “If all players pass in succession (that is, if all players pass without taking any actions in between passing), the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
    CR 701.7a — “To create one or more tokens with certain characteristics, put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield.”
    CR 603.2c — “An ability triggers only once each time its trigger event occurs. However, it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences.”
  5. The precision that separates this drainer from stacks 002 and 003: a counter-KILLED Insect pays Bats NOTHING. When a -1/-1 counter drops a 1/1 Insect to 0/0 it is put into its owner's graveyard as a state-based action (704.5f). That is a death, and 002/003's drainers meter exactly that. It is not a sacrifice: to sacrifice is for the permanent's CONTROLLER to move it to the graveyard (701.21a), an action a player takes to pay a cost or follow an instruction, whereas 704.5f is the game putting it there automatically. Bats' condition is 'create or sacrifice', so a counter-kill matches neither half. Only the creation half applies to those tokens -- they paid Bats once, when they were made.
    Established for the whole engine: every token is worth +1 Bats trigger when created, +1 more only if it is SACRIFICED (to Yawgmoth's cost), and 0 for dying to a counter. Bats is therefore not a strictly better Zulaport Cutthroat; it counts a different set of events.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
  6. Establish the engine's bound before running it, and answer the 2/3 question. Each activation spends one body (the sacrifice) and Nest returns at most one, so the loop is body-neutral only while a counter can land on a creature that is NOT one of your own Insects. The available counter sinks are: (i) Grizzly Bears, an opponent's creature -- two counter-placement events, the second killing it, and both are free to you because it is not your body; (ii) Mirkwood Bats itself. THIS IS WHERE THE 2/3 CHANGES THE BOUND. A 1/1 drainer (stack 003's) dies to its first counter and can never be a sink. Bats at 2/3 absorbs TWO -1/-1 counters and is 0/1 -- toughness 1, above 0, so 704.5f does not apply and it keeps draining (122.1a). Those two counters are two extra body-neutral counter events, each worth a free Nest token and therefore a free Bats trigger. A third counter would make Bats -1/0 and kill it, so two is the cap. (iii) Yawgmoth cannot be a sink at all: he is a Human Cleric and has protection from Humans, so his own ability -- an ability from a Human source -- can never target him (702.16b). And 'up to one target creature' permits choosing zero targets (115.6), so the pilot is never forced to put a counter anywhere.
    Counter-sink budget: Bears 2 events, Mirkwood Bats 2 events, then nothing but your own Insects (each such event kills a token and pays Bats nothing). The loop is strictly FINITE. Bats being a 2/3 rather than a 1/1 is worth +5 Bats triggers on this board, measured against the same Bats text on a 1/1 body: a 1/1 drainer dies to its first -1/-1 counter and can never be a counter sink, and that board tops out at 8 triggers where this one reaches 13. The +5 enumerates as +2 on the SACRIFICE half and +3 on the CREATION half, which is where it stops looking like a coincidence. THE +2 SACRIFICES, AND THE MECHANISM IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE: Bats' first counter slot buys one body-neutral activation; its second slot makes the proliferate worth a second Nest token, which funds the other activation. Only the FIRST of Bats' two counters is a counter an activation aimed at it; the second arrives from the proliferate, which can only grow a permanent that ALREADY carries a counter (step 8), and no Insect of yours can ever carry a -1/-1 counter and live -- so that second counter never had an Insect alternative to be spared from. Nor does this line spare Insects at all: it counter-kills THREE of your own tokens (D, F and H, step 9) where the 1/1 board's best line kills two, and three is forced in every optimal line here, not a choice. What the 2/3 body buys is ACTIVATIONS, not bodies -- the engine therefore runs SIX Yawgmoth activations instead of four, and every activation pays the sacrifice half exactly once, because sacrificing a token IS this ability's cost. THE +3 CREATIONS: two of them are the Nest tokens those two extra activations make (five token-creating activations here against three, since the last activation in either line targets nothing and makes nothing), and the third is the proliferate -- Bats carrying counters gives proliferate a SECOND permanent to grow, so one proliferate yields two Nest triggers on this board against one on the 1/1 board. None of the +5 comes from the counter-killed tokens: those pay the creation half only and are never sacrificed (steps 5 and 9), so no token this line kills with a counter is counted twice.
    CR 702.16b — “A permanent or player with protection can’t be targeted by spells with the stated quality and can’t be targeted by abilities from a source with the stated quality.”
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 122.1a — “-X/-Y counters subtract from power and toughness.”
  7. Activation #2, spending the first of Bats' own two counter slots. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect B (a token) -- Bats triggers, and by step 1's sequencing that trigger goes on the stack above the ability and resolves first. Target: Mirkwood Bats itself (a legal target -- it has no protection or hexproof, and you may target your own creature). Bats trigger resolves; then the ability resolves, putting a -1/-1 counter on Bats (2/3 -> 1/2, alive) and drawing a card. SBAs are performed and only then is the resulting Nest trigger put on the stack (117.5); nothing dies. Nest resolves, creating Insect D, and Bats triggers on the creation.
    Two Bats triggers this activation, exactly as (a) says. Opponents 38 -> 36 each; you 39 -> 38 (1 life paid, no gain). Library 58, hand 2. Bats 1/2 with one -1/-1 counter. Bodies: Insect C, Insect D. Bats triggers so far: 4. Table life removed: 16.
    CR 117.5 — “then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  8. Now spend the three Swamps the scenario deliberately supplies -- Yawgmoth's -1/-1 ability costs no mana, so the only mana sink is his second ability: '{B}{B}, Discard a card: Proliferate.' Tap two Swamps, discard a card drawn earlier. Proliferate lets you CHOOSE any number of permanents that have a counter and give each one more of a kind already there (701.34a), so you choose exactly Grizzly Bears and Mirkwood Bats and nothing else. Bears goes to two counters (0/0); Bats goes to two counters (0/1, alive). This is one event that puts a -1/-1 counter on TWO different creatures, so Nest of Scarabs -- 'whenever you put one or more -1/-1 counters on A CREATURE' -- triggers twice, once per creature, by 603.2c's multiple-occurrences clause. SBAs run before those triggers go on the stack: Bears is 0/0 and is put into its OWNER's graveyard (704.5f); Bats at 0/1 survives. The two Nest triggers then resolve one at a time, each creating one token, each paying Bats once.
    Two tokens created (Insect E, Insect F) for two mana and a discard, with NO sacrifice spent -- two Bats triggers for free. Opponents 36 -> 34 each; you 38, hand 1, library 58. Grizzly Bears is dead and in opponent A's graveyard; its death pays Bats nothing (step 5). Bats 0/1 with two counters -- its sink capacity is now exhausted. Bodies: Insects C, D, E, F. One Swamp untapped, which can no longer buy anything (proliferate costs {B}{B}). Bats triggers so far: 6. Table life removed: 24.
    CR 701.34a — “To proliferate means to choose any number of permanents and/or players that have a counter, then give each one additional counter of each kind that permanent or player already has.”
    CR 603.2c — “it can trigger repeatedly if one event contains multiple occurrences”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  9. The terminal phase: every external sink is gone, so each remaining activation must aim its counter at one of your own Insects (or at nothing). Aiming it at an Insect is strictly better than aiming at nothing -- the counter-kill itself pays Bats nothing (step 5), but it triggers Nest, and the token Nest makes IS a creation, worth one Bats trigger. So each of these activations is still worth TWO Bats triggers (sacrifice + creation) while costing a net one body. Run it three times. Activation #3: pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect C (Bats trigger), target Insect D; D becomes 0/0, SBAs put it in the graveyard and it then ceases to exist, and only then does the Nest trigger go on the stack (117.5, 704.5f); Nest creates Insect G (Bats trigger). Activation #4: sacrifice Insect E, target Insect F, Nest creates Insect H -- identical shape. Activation #5: sacrifice Insect G, target Insect H, Nest creates Insect I -- identical shape.
    Six more Bats triggers across three activations. Opponents 34 -> 28 each; you 38 -> 35 (three more life paid). Library 58 -> 55, hand 4. Bodies fall 4 -> 3 -> 2 -> 1: only Insect I remains. Bats triggers so far: 12. Table life removed: 48. Note the shape: each of these activations kills two of your tokens and makes one, so the engine is now net -1 body per activation and is visibly winding down.
    CR 117.5 — “then repeats this process until no state-based actions are performed. Then triggered abilities are put on the stack”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  10. Activation #6, the last one, and the two lines the pilot must decline. Pay 1 life, sacrifice Insect I -- a token, so Bats triggers one final time. For the counter, the only remaining creatures are Yawgmoth (untargetable by his own ability, step 6) and Mirkwood Bats at 0/1. Targeting Bats would be a THIRD counter on it: Bats becomes -1/0 and is put into the graveyard by state-based action before the resulting Nest trigger is ever put on the stack (704.5f), so the token that trigger creates would be created with no Bats on the battlefield and would pay nothing -- the option is worth exactly zero and costs the drainer. The pilot therefore chooses zero targets, which 'up to one target creature' expressly permits (115.6): no counter, no Nest trigger, no token. The engine also cannot continue past this: the only creatures left are Yawgmoth, who cannot pay his own cost ('Sacrifice ANOTHER creature'), and Bats, which is not a token -- sacrificing Bats would pay Bats nothing and remove the drainer, so it is declined too.
    One final Bats trigger. Opponents 28 -> 27 each; you 35 -> 34. Library 54, hand 5. No tokens remain. Bats triggers: 13. Table life removed: 52.
    CR 115.6 — “A spell or ability that requires targets may allow zero targets to be chosen.”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  11. ANSWER (b) and the final state-based-action check. Ledger of Bats triggers, by half. SACRIFICE half -- six tokens were sacrificed to Yawgmoth's cost: Insect A (pre-resolution, step 1), B, C, E, G, I. CREATION half -- seven tokens were created by Nest of Scarabs: C and D from the two activations that used an external/Bats sink, E and F from the single proliferate (two Nest triggers), and G, H, I from the three terminal activations. Six plus seven is THIRTEEN Bats triggers, each draining every opponent for 1 (119.3). Note what did NOT pay: Grizzly Bears' death and the three Insects killed by counters (D, F, H) are four deaths worth nothing to Bats -- but only THREE of those four are what a 'you control' death-metering drainer would have banked, because Grizzly Bears is an opponent's creature. Zulaport Cutthroat (stack 003) reads 'Whenever this creature or another creature you control dies' and Bastion of Remembrance (stack 002) reads 'Whenever a creature you control dies'; the Bears is not a creature you control, so its death does not match either ability's trigger event and neither ability triggers on it (603.2); the word 'you' on each of those objects refers to that object's controller -- you -- and not to opponent A (109.5). They bank the three counter-killed Insects D, F and H, which are tokens you control. The one drainer that would see all four is stack 004's Blood Artist, which is controller-agnostic -- 'Whenever this creature or another creature dies' carries no controller restriction, so the Bears' death does pay it (though it drains one 'target player', not each opponent). Final SBA check: every opponent is at 27, well above 0, so no player loses the game (704.5a); you are at 34 and drew only 6 cards from a 60-card library. Every Insect token that reached a graveyard, whether sacrificed or counter-killed, then ceased to exist (704.5d), which is why nine token deaths leave no trace in any graveyard.
    THIRTEEN Bats triggers. Each opponent loses 13 life: 40 -> 27. Total removed from the table: 13 x 4 = 52. Opposing life 160 -> 108 (32.5% of the table). THE COMPARISON, STATED PER SIBLING, BECAUSE THE TWO SIBLING BOARDS ARE NOT THE SAME BOARD. LIKE-FOR-LIKE AGAINST STACK 003: 003 is this exact three-body board with the drainer slot filled by Zulaport Cutthroat, and its checker-passed 7 drains / 28 opposing life is a WITH-proliferate figure, exactly as this line's is -- so this is 13 drains vs 7 and 52 pod life vs 28, +24 life, 86% more. STACK 002 IS A DIFFERENT BOARD AND ITS 28 IS NOT COMPARABLE TO THIS LINE: 002 carries a fourth body, the 1/1 white Human Soldier token that Bastion of Remembrance's own enters-the-battlefield ability creates (an enchantment cannot be a body), and its headline 7 drains / 28 life is its explicit NO-proliferate board invariant while this line spends the proliferate. 002's comparable with-proliferate figure is 8 drains / 32 opposing life, against which this is 13 drains vs 8 and 52 pod life vs 32, +20 life, +62.5%. Both comparisons favour Mirkwood Bats and neither is the other; 002, 003 and this stack are three boards and must never be presented as one result on one board. The margin's reason is structural, not incidental: a creation-and-sacrifice drainer is paid twice by a single Yawgmoth activation, whereas a death drainer is paid once per body, and Bats' 2/3 body adds two free counter sinks that a 1/1 drainer cannot offer. The price is your own life total: Bats gives you nothing back, so you finish at 34 rather than the 42 that 003's Zulaport Cutthroat -- which gains you 1 life per drain -- left you at (002 ends at 42 on its no-proliferate invariant and 43 on its proliferate line). NOT LETHAL, and not close.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 704.5d — “If a token is in a zone other than the battlefield, it ceases to exist.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 109.5 — “The words “you” and “your” on an object refer to the object’s controller”

A-009 Warren Soultrader + Gravecrawler + Bastion of Remembrance ✓ cleared, ? cycle(s) · 14 steps · 53 citations
Rules version —
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then resolve one full further iteration of the loop. (1) Can the loop be repeated an unbounded number of times from this board with no mana or life from outside it — specifically, does casting Gravecrawler from the graveyard have a legal permission each time, and does the Treasure created by each activation pay for that cast? (2) What is the exact net change per completed iteration to your life total, to each opponent's life total, and to the number of Treasures you control?
  1. Establish the stack order before anyone passes priority. Warren Soultrader's activated ability was put on the stack first (pos 0); its costs — pay 1 life, sacrifice another creature — were then paid, and Gravecrawler's death during that cost payment triggered Bastion of Remembrance. A triggered ability does nothing at the moment it triggers; it is put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority, i.e. after activation is complete, so it lands ON TOP of the ability that caused it (pos 1).
    Stack from bottom to top: [0] Warren Soultrader's ability (create a Treasure token), [1] Bastion of Remembrance's trigger (each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life). You are at 39 (the 1 life is already paid). Battlefield bodies: Warren Soultrader only — Gravecrawler is in your graveyard, not on the battlefield. Opponents: 40 / 40 / 40 / 40.
    CR 602.2a — “That ability is created on the stack as an object that’s not a card. It becomes the topmost object on the stack.”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving.”
    CR 603.3 — “Once an ability has triggered, its controller puts it on the stack as an object that’s not a card the next time a player would receive priority.”
    CR 405.2 — “Each time an object is put on the stack, it’s put on top of all objects already there.”
  2. Confirm the cost payment that produced this stack was legal, and locate Gravecrawler. Gravecrawler is a creature you control other than Warren Soultrader, so it satisfies 'another creature'; sacrificing it moved it from the battlefield directly to its owner's graveyard. You had 40 life and the life component of the cost was 1, so that payment was legal.
    Gravecrawler is in your graveyard and no longer on the battlefield; your only body is Warren Soultrader. Your life is 39. (Whether the graveyard copy can be cast again is not decided here — that permission is established at step 6.)
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 119.4 — “If a cost or effect allows a player to pay an amount of life greater than 0, the player may do so only if their life total is greater than or equal to the amount of the payment.”
    CR 118.3b — “Paying life is done by subtracting the indicated amount of life from a player’s life total.”
  3. All players pass priority. The TOP object resolves first: Bastion of Remembrance's triggered ability.
    Each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life. Per opponent: 40 → 39, for all four seats. Across the pod that is 4 life removed in total (160 → 156 opposing life) — the per-seat figure (1) and the pod figure (4) are not interchangeable. You: 39 → 40.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. State-based actions are checked before any player receives priority again. No player is at 0 or less life (you 40; opponents 39 each), no creature has lethal damage, and nothing else on this board qualifies. No state-based action is performed and no ability has triggered, so the active player (you) receives priority with Warren Soultrader's ability still on the stack.
    No state-based action occurs. You hold priority; the Soultrader ability is the only object on the stack.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”

Establish here, once, why nothing further triggers — this is what caps the drain at exactly 1 per iteration. A triggered ability triggers only when a game event or game state MATCHES its trigger event. Bastion of Remembrance has exactly two triggered abilities: 'When this enchantment enters, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token' (its entry is long past) and 'Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life'. Bastion has no trigger event of the form 'a permanent enters' or 'a creature enters' at all, so creating a token — of any kind — matches neither ability. Creating the Treasure is therefore not a trigger event for Bastion, and the same rule governs steps 7 and 8 below.
    You create one Treasure token, an artifact token you control with "{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color." No ability triggers on its creation. Because the Treasure is an artifact and not a creature, its later sacrifice will not be a creature you control dying either, so it will not match Bastion's death trigger (applied at step 7). Treasures: 0 → 1. The stack is now empty. STACK AS GIVEN IS FULLY RESOLVED: you 40, each opponent 39, Treasures 1, Gravecrawler in graveyard, Warren Soultrader and Bastion of Remembrance on the battlefield.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 701.7a — “put the specified number of tokens with the specified characteristics onto the battlefield”
    CR 111.2 — “The player who creates a token is its owner. The token enters the battlefield under that player’s control.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  6. ITERATION 1, part (1) of the question — permission to cast Gravecrawler from the graveyard. Before you receive that priority the game checks state-based actions and performs none: with the stack empty the board is you at 40, each opponent at 39, Warren Soultrader and Bastion of Remembrance on the battlefield and the newly created Treasure on the battlefield as well, so no listed condition is met and no triggered ability is waiting to be put on the stack; the check therefore performs nothing and priority is handed back. You then receive priority with an empty stack in your precombat main phase; the phase does not end merely because the stack emptied. Gravecrawler's own static ability reads 'You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as you control a Zombie.' Two rules are needed, not one. 113.6f is the rule that makes an ability of this kind — one that modifies which zones that object can be cast from — function everywhere, including from the graveyard; without it, appealing to 604.6 alone would be circular, since the graveyard is a zone you could cast Gravecrawler from ONLY because of this very ability. 604.6 then describes the ordinary operation of such a static ability in that zone, and 601.3 is what the permission is needed for. The Zombie condition is met by Warren Soultrader, whose printed type line is Creature — Zombie Goblin Wizard; Soultrader is never sacrificed by this loop (its own ability requires sacrificing ANOTHER creature), so the permission is available on every iteration.
    Casting Gravecrawler from the graveyard is legal now and on every future iteration: a noninstant spell, your main phase, stack empty, priority held, permission granted by the card's own ability, which functions in the graveyard by 113.6f, with the Zombie requirement satisfied by the untouched Warren Soultrader.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 601.3 — “A player can begin to cast a spell only if a rule or effect allows that player to cast it and no rule or effect prohibits that player from casting it.”
    CR 113.6f — “An object’s ability that restricts or modifies what zones that particular object can be played or cast from functions everywhere, even outside the game.”
    CR 604.6 — “Some static abilities apply while a card is in any zone that you could cast or play it from (usually your hand).”
    CR 117.1a — “A player may cast a noninstant spell during their main phase any time they have priority and the stack is empty.”
    CR 500.2 — “A phase or step in which players receive priority ends when the stack is empty and all players pass in succession.”
  7. ITERATION 1, part (1) continued — does the Treasure pay for the cast? Gravecrawler's mana cost is {B}. First establish that the Treasure's ability, '{T}, Sacrifice this token: Add one mana of any color', qualifies as a mana ability: it is an activated ability, it requires no target, it could add mana to your mana pool when it resolves, and it is not a loyalty ability — the three criteria of 605.1a, all met. Because it IS a mana ability, you may activate it while casting Gravecrawler, before costs are paid, and it does not use the stack. The Treasure is an artifact token, not a creature, so the summoning-sickness restriction — written only for a creature's activated ability with the tap symbol — does not reach it even though it was created this turn. Sacrificing it is not a creature you control dying, so no game event matches Bastion's trigger event (the rule stated at step 5) and Bastion does not trigger.

The sacrificed Treasure is a TOKEN now in a zone other than the battlefield, so it ceases to exist — this is the one state-based action this loop actually performs, and it happens at the next check, i.e. when a player would next receive priority once the cast is complete (the check stated at step 8). Nothing in part (2)'s ledger turns on that timing: the number of Treasures you control fell to 0 the moment the token left the battlefield. The same rule's parenthetical is why the trigger analysis above is safe — any applicable triggered ability would trigger BEFORE the token ceased to exist, and none is applicable, because Bastion's trigger event is a creature you control dying. No Bastion trigger. Gravecrawler is on the stack. Exactly one Treasure funds exactly one cast — the mana ledger of one iteration balances at zero.
    CR 605.1a — “An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn’t require a target (see rule 115.6), it could add mana to a player’s mana pool when it resolves, and it’s not a loyalty ability.”
    CR 601.2g — “the player then has a chance to activate mana abilities”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 302.6 — “A creature’s activated ability with the tap symbol or the untap symbol in its activation cost can’t be activated unless the creature has been under its controller’s control continuously since their most recent turn began.”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 106.4 — “When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player’s mana pool. From there, it can be used to pay costs immediately”
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 111.7 — “A token that’s in a zone other than the battlefield ceases to exist. This is a state-based action”

It is a new object with no relation to the card that was in the graveyard. Nothing dies during this cast or resolution, so no game event matches Bastion's 'Whenever a creature you control dies' trigger event, and Bastion does not trigger.
    Gravecrawler (2/1 Zombie) is on the battlefield. Bodies: Warren Soultrader + Gravecrawler = 2. Treasures 0. No Bastion trigger.     CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”

    You: 40 → 39. Gravecrawler moves from the battlefield to your graveyard — it dies, and it is a card, so it rests on top of that graveyard. Soultrader's ability is on the stack.

  14. ANSWER TO PART (1) — is the loop unbounded from this board with no outside mana or life? Every resource an iteration consumes it also replaces, in the same iteration, from inside the loop: the {B} for Gravecrawler comes from the Treasure the previous activation made, whose ability is a mana ability (steps 5, 7, 12); the 1 life for Soultrader comes back from Bastion (steps 9, 11); Gravecrawler is a card, so it returns to the graveyard each time and its own ability functions there (steps 6, 9); the Zombie that grants the graveyard permission is Warren Soultrader, which the loop never sacrifices (step 6). Nothing in the loop draws on the three tapped Swamps, the empty hand, or the library. The loop is therefore repeatable an unbounded number of times, and because it can be repeated indefinitely the shortcut rules let you declare a number of iterations rather than perform them.
    YES on both halves of part (1): the graveyard cast has a legal permission on every iteration, and each activation's Treasure exactly funds the next cast. The loop is self-funding and unbounded in the rules sense; in practice it terminates because the drain is not — the operative threshold is 0 life, so 40 drain events bring a 40-life opponent to exactly 0, and one drain has already resolved on the stack as given, leaving 39 further iterations after step 5 to put all four opponents at 0 simultaneously, whereupon each of them loses the game. 'Infinite lifeloss' as a containment tag is CONFIRMED for this board, with the arithmetic that matters: 1 life per opponent per iteration, 4 across the pod per iteration, 40 drain events to kill each seat.
    CR 732.1b — “the shortcut rules can be used to determine how many times those actions are repeated without having to actually perform them, and how the loop is broken”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”

A-010 Mikaeus + Yawgmoth ✓ cleared, ? cycle(s) · 10 steps · 35 citations
Rules version —
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack. When Yawgmoth's ability puts a -1/-1 counter on Gravecrawler, which already carries a +1/+1 counter, what does the next state-based-action check do to those counters — and is Gravecrawler afterwards eligible for undying the next time it dies? Then state what bounds repetition of this loop.
  1. No player responds; priority passes and the stack resolves top-down (last in, first out). The topmost object is pos 2, the undying triggered ability granted to Viscera Seer by Mikaeus. Below it sit pos 1 (Bastion of Remembrance's death trigger) and pos 0 (Yawgmoth's activated ability, both of whose costs — 1 life, sacrifice Viscera Seer — are already paid).
    Resolution order is: undying trigger, then Bastion trigger, then Yawgmoth's ability. Viscera Seer is in the graveyard and is NOT a body on the battlefield; the board entering this resolution is three creatures — Yawgmoth 2/4, Mikaeus 5/5, Gravecrawler (2/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus, one +1/+1 counter = 4/3).
    CR 608.1 — “Each time all players pass in succession, the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves.”
    CR 117.4 — “the spell or ability on top of the stack resolves”
  2. The undying trigger resolves. Undying is a leaves-the-battlefield ability with an intervening 'if' clause: 'if it had no +1/+1 counters on it'. Viscera Seer is now a new object in the graveyard, so the condition is evaluated against the creature as it last existed ON THE BATTLEFIELD — where it had no counters at all (it was 1/1 printed, 2/2 only because of Mikaeus's static +1/+1, which is not a counter).
    The condition is true both at trigger time and again on resolution, so the ability resolves: Viscera Seer returns to the battlefield under its owner's control with a +1/+1 counter on it. It returns as a new object with no memory of its previous existence.
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 603.4 — “If the ability triggers, it checks the stated condition again as it resolves. If the condition isn’t true at that time, the ability is removed from the stack and does nothing.”
    CR 603.10a — “Some zone-change triggers look back in time. These are leaves-the-battlefield abilities”
    CR 608.2h — “if it’s no longer in that zone, or if the effect has moved it from a public zone to a hidden zone, the effect uses the object’s last known information”
    CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”
  3. Before any player receives priority again, state-based actions are checked. The returned Viscera Seer is 1/1 printed with one +1/+1 counter and Mikaeus's +1/+1 static bonus, both applied in layer 7c, so it is 3/3. Its toughness is above 0 and no other SBA condition applies.
    No state-based action is performed. The board is now four bodies: Yawgmoth 2/4, Mikaeus 5/5, Gravecrawler 4/3 (one +1/+1 counter), Viscera Seer 3/3 (one +1/+1 counter). Because Viscera Seer now HAS a +1/+1 counter, it is not eligible for undying if it dies again in this state.
    CR 117.5 — “Each time a player would get priority, the game first performs all applicable state-based actions as a single event”
    CR 613.4c — “Layer 7c: Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness (but don’t set power and/or toughness to a specific number or value) are applied.”
  4. Bastion of Remembrance's trigger resolves: 'each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' It triggered on Viscera Seer's death during cost payment.
    PER SEAT: each of the four opponents loses 1 life, 40 -> 39. POD TOTAL: 4 life removed across the pod, 160 -> 156. These are not interchangeable figures. You gain 1 life: 39 -> 40. No opponent is at 0 or less, so no player loses the game on the following state-based-action check.
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  5. Yawgmoth's activated ability resolves. Its target, Gravecrawler, never left the battlefield and its characteristics have not changed in any way that matters to 'up to one target creature', so the target is still legal. The ability puts a -1/-1 counter on Gravecrawler and you draw a card.
    Gravecrawler now carries one +1/+1 counter AND one -1/-1 counter. Its power/toughness at this instant: 2/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus and the two counters all applied in layer 7c, netting 3/2. You draw one card: hand 0 -> 1, library 60 -> 59.
    CR 608.2b — “If the spell or ability specifies targets, it checks whether the targets are still legal.”
    CR 121.1 — “A player draws a card by putting the top card of their library into their hand.”
    CR 613.4c — “Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness”
  6. THE CRUX. The stack is now empty and you would receive priority, so state-based actions are checked first. Gravecrawler has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it.
    N = 1 (the smaller of the two counts), so one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter are removed from Gravecrawler simultaneously as a state-based action. Gravecrawler ends the check with ZERO counters of either kind, at 3/2 (2/1 printed plus Mikaeus's static +1/+1). The 0-or-less-toughness SBA does NOT apply: its toughness is 2. The -1/-1 counter therefore did not shrink Gravecrawler at all in net terms — it annihilated the +1/+1 counter and both were removed.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5q — “If a permanent has both a +1/+1 counter and a -1/-1 counter on it, N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it.”
    CR 122.3 — “as a state-based action, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard.”
  7. Undying eligibility for Gravecrawler is re-armed. Gravecrawler is a Zombie (non-Human), so Mikaeus continues to grant it undying. Undying's intervening 'if' clause asks whether it had no +1/+1 counters on it, evaluated against the creature as it last existed on the battlefield.
    YES — Gravecrawler is eligible for undying the next time it dies. It now has no +1/+1 counters, so if it is sacrificed in this state the condition is true both when the trigger would go on the stack and again on resolution, and it returns with a fresh +1/+1 counter. Symmetrically, Viscera Seer is currently NOT eligible: it carries the +1/+1 counter from step 2 until something removes it. Exactly one of the two non-Human bodies is armed at any time, and Yawgmoth's -1/-1 counter is what flips which one.
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 603.4 — “it checks the stated condition again as it resolves”
    CR 608.2h — “the effect uses the object’s last known information”
  8. THE SWAP, VERIFIED ON THE OTHER BODY. Each further iteration is one activation of Yawgmoth: pay 1 life, sacrifice the body that currently carries NO +1/+1 counter (it returns via undying with one), and put the -1/-1 counter on the OTHER body. On the very first swap that other body is VISCERA SEER, which is printed 1/1 — smaller than Gravecrawler — so the step-6 state-based-action check must be re-performed for it rather than assumed. At that check Viscera Seer is 1/1 printed, +1/+1 from Mikaeus, one +1/+1 counter and one -1/-1 counter, all in layer 7c: power 1+1+1-1 = 2, toughness 1+1+1-1 = 2.
    Toughness is 2, not 0 or less, so the creature-death state-based action does NOT apply to Viscera Seer and it survives the check. In that same single state-based-action event established in step 6, N = 1 counter of each kind is removed, leaving Viscera Seer a 2/2 with ZERO counters — re-armed for undying exactly as Gravecrawler was in step 6, while Gravecrawler now carries the +1/+1 counter from its own undying return. The alternation therefore holds in both directions: the pair swaps roles every activation, and neither body ever dies to the counter itself.
    CR 704.5f — “If a creature has toughness 0 or less, it’s put into its owner’s graveyard. Regeneration can’t replace this event.”
    CR 704.5q — “N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 613.4c — “Effects and counters that modify power and/or toughness”
  9. WHAT BOUNDS REPETITION, AND THE ONE TRAP ON THIS BOARD. First, that the line repeats at all: after step 6's state-based-action check the stack is empty, it is your precombat main phase and priority returns to you, and a player may activate an activated ability any time they have priority. Yawgmoth's ability carries no mana symbol in its cost and no activation restriction, so it may be activated again at {0} available — that is what makes steps 8-10 repetition claims rather than a one-shot. Three quantities then bound the repetition, and the one further free sacrifice outlet on this board is a TRAP, not a bonus.
    (1) LIFE is not the bound: you pay 1 life per activation and Bastion returns exactly 1 life per creature death, so your total is flat at 40 and the 119.4 requirement that you have at least the life you are paying is never threatened. (2) DRAIN is the clock: one creature dies per activation, so each activation removes 1 life PER OPPONENT and 4 across the pod. From the post-resolution 39 per seat, 39 further activations put every opponent at 0 and they lose to state-based actions; that is 156 pod life removed. (3) The LIBRARY is the hard bound and it is finite: Yawgmoth's ability reads 'Put a -1/-1 counter on up to one target creature and draw a card' with no 'may', and its controller follows those instructions in the order written, so the draw is MANDATORY and cannot be declined. With 59 cards left you may activate at most 59 more times; a 60th activation would attempt a draw from an empty library and YOU lose the game at the next state-based-action check. The loop is therefore NOT infinite — a 59-activation engine that needs 39, with 20 activations of margin. (4) THE SECOND OUTLET IS A TRAP — CORRECTED FROM THE PREVIOUS PASS, WHICH CALLED IT 'STRICTLY FAVOURABLE'. Viscera Seer is back on the battlefield from step 2 and has its own activated ability, 'Sacrifice a creature: Scry 1' — an activated ability written cost-then-effect whose cost contains no mana symbol, so it is free even at {0} available, and each use kills a creature and so triggers Bastion for another 1 per seat while consuming no library. READ THE COST EXACTLY, BECAUSE THE PREVIOUS PASS DID NOT: the Seer's cost is 'Sacrifice A creature', NOT 'another creature' as Yawgmoth's cost is. The Seer may therefore eat ANY creature you control — including Mikaeus, including Yawgmoth, and including ITSELF. That distinction is what makes the finisher count 5 rather than 3. It is nevertheless ENGINE-ENDING if used mid-loop, and a pilot who reads 'free extra drain' and fires it kills their own combo. WHY: the engine's steady state is exactly one ARMED body (no +1/+1 counter, undying-eligible) and one LOADED body (one +1/+1 counter, not eligible), and only Yawgmoth's -1/-1 counter can strip a +1/+1 counter — it does so via the step-6 state-based action, one counter per activation, which is why one Yawgmoth activation re-arms exactly one body. The Seer's ability places no counter at all. So if you sacrifice the ARMED body to the Seer, undying returns it WITH a +1/+1 counter and now BOTH bodies are loaded and NEITHER is undying-eligible; and if you sacrifice the LOADED body to the Seer, its undying trigger's intervening 'if' is false and that body is gone PERMANENTLY. THE TWO BRANCHES DIFFER IN WHERE THE PERMANENT LOSS FALLS, AND THE PREVIOUS PASS'S 'either way the next Yawgmoth activation must sacrifice a counter-bearing body' IS FALSE IN THE SECOND: in branch A (armed body eaten) nothing is lost yet, both bodies carry counters, and the NEXT Yawgmoth activation must sacrifice a counter-bearing body, which dies for good; in branch B (loaded body eaten) the permanent loss has ALREADY happened and the survivor is ARMED, so the next Yawgmoth sacrifice loses nothing permanently — it returns loaded. THE CONCLUSION IS UNCHANGED, because both branches converge on the same state, ONE alternation body, and one body can never sustain the alternation. An activation kills exactly one body and yields exactly one -1/-1 counter, which strips exactly one +1/+1 counter, and it can never strip the counter on the body it just ate: that body was sacrificed to pay the cost, so if you had targeted it, on resolution it is no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted and is an illegal target; and if you targeted anything else, its +1/+1 counter simply stays. Re-arming therefore always costs a DIFFERENT body's death, and with one alternation body left the only other creatures are Mikaeus — whose death removes undying from that body entirely, since the grant is his — and Yawgmoth, which its own 'another creature' cost may not sacrifice. The alternation is over within one or two further activations in both branches. ONE mid-loop Seer sacrifice therefore buys 1 drain per seat and costs you roughly 34 of the 39 activations you still need: you collect about five drains from the collapsing board instead of the 39 the intact alternation would have delivered. THE ONLY SAFE USE IS AS A FINISHER, after the alternation is no longer needed — and at that point at most FIVE free Seer sacrifices exist in total, CORRECTED FROM THREE, which omitted Mikaeus and Yawgmoth. The finisher board is exactly four bodies: Yawgmoth, Mikaeus, and the two alternation bodies, one armed and one loaded, one of which is the Seer itself. Order it so the SEER EATS ITSELF LAST, since its outlet leaves the battlefield with it: (1) sacrifice the ARMED body — it dies for a Bastion drain and undying returns it LOADED, no permanent loss; (2)-(4) sacrifice, in any order, the three bodies other than the Seer that are now on the battlefield — the loaded alternation body (its intervening 'if' is false, permanent), Mikaeus (his own grant reads 'OTHER non-Human creatures you control get +1/+1 and have undying', so it excludes himself and he has no undying, permanent) and Yawgmoth (a Legendary Creature — Human Cleric per cards.json, so Mikaeus's non-Human grant never reached it, permanent); (5) sacrifice the Seer to itself, permanent. Five deaths, five Bastion drains, zero library consumed. The count is the same 5 in EITHER orientation of which alternation body is armed: if the Seer is the armed body, it is step (1) and it comes straight back to keep working. SIX IS IMPOSSIBLE: four bodies plus exactly ONE undying return, because only one body is armed at a time and neither Yawgmoth (Human) nor Mikaeus (excluded by his own 'Other') can undy. ACCOUNTING CONSEQUENCE: margin = 59 - (39 - k) = 20 + k, where k is the number of finisher Seer sacrifices taken and 0 <= k <= 5. The margin is 20 activations clean and at most 25 with all five finisher sacrifices — a bounded range, NOT an open-ended lower bound as an earlier pass asserted, and NOT the 20-23 the previous pass computed from a count of three. Nothing else on this board caps the loop: the loop costs no mana, Gravecrawler cannot be recast from the graveyard because that requires {B} and mana available is {0}, and Mikaeus need only remain on the battlefield, which it does because Yawgmoth's cost sacrifices 'another creature' and you always choose one of the two designated bodies.
    CR 117.1b — “A player may activate an activated ability any time they have priority.”
    CR 119.4 — “If a player pays life, the payment is subtracted from their life total; in other words, the player loses that much life.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 608.2c — “The controller of the spell or ability follows its instructions in the order written.”
    CR 704.5b — “If a player attempted to draw a card from a library with no cards in it since the last time state-based actions were checked, that player loses the game.”
    CR 602.1 — “Activated abilities have a cost and an effect. They are written as “[Cost]: [Effect.] [Activation instructions (if any).]””
    CR 702.93a — “if it had no +1/+1 counters on it, return it to the battlefield under its owner’s control with a +1/+1 counter on it”
    CR 704.5q — “N +1/+1 and N -1/-1 counters are removed from it, where N is the smaller of the number of +1/+1 and -1/-1 counters on it”
    CR 608.2b — “A target that’s no longer in the zone it was in when it was targeted is illegal.”
  10. HOW THE KILL BECOMES A WIN. The 39th further activation brings every opponent from 1 to 0 life; the drain is one simultaneous event per Bastion resolution, so all four seats reach 0 together and all four lose the game at the next state-based-action check. You are still in the game at that moment: your life is flat at 40 (step 9, item 1), and you have used 39 of the 59 draws the library permits, so the deck-out state-based action has not been met.
    With all four of your opponents having left the game and you still in it, you win the game immediately. This is the rule that converts four simultaneous losses in a five-player pod into a win; the loss of the opponents alone does not state it. The Mikaeus + Yawgmoth line is a verified kill on this board.
    CR 104.2a — “A player still in the game wins the game if that player’s opponents have all left the game. This happens immediately and overrides all effects that would preclude that player from winning the game.”
After the line
Your life
40
Your battlefield
Yawgmoth, Thran Physician (2/4, commander), Mikaeus, the Unhallowed (5/5), Gravecrawler (3/2 — 2/1 printed +1/+1 from Mikaeus; NO counters; undying re-armed — this is the ARMED body), Viscera Seer (3/3 — 1/1 printed, one +1/+1 counter, +1/+1 from Mikaeus; undying NOT available while that counter remains — this is the LOADED body; its own free sacrifice outlet is live but is a TRAP mid-loop, see step 9 item 4; at most FIVE free Seer sacrifices exist as a finisher), Bastion of Remembrance, Swamp (tapped) x4
Opponents

A-011 Gravecrawler + Phyrexian Altar + Carrion Feeder + Bastion of Remembrance ✓ cleared, ? cycle(s) · 11 steps · 34 citations
Rules version —
The question put to the record
Resolve the stack, then resolve one full further iteration. Can the loop be repeated an unbounded number of times from this board with no mana and no life from outside it, and what is the exact net change per completed iteration to your life total and to each opponent's life total?
  1. Establish what is on the stack, and check it against the corrected scenario. Phyrexian Altar's ability — 'Sacrifice a creature: Add one mana of any color.' — requires no target, could add mana when it resolves, and is not a loyalty ability, so it is a mana ability. A mana ability never uses the stack: it resolved immediately when it was activated and put {B} into your mana pool, where it stays as unspent mana until the end of the step or phase. Bastion of Remembrance's ability triggered on Gravecrawler's death, and that death happened DURING the payment of the Altar's activation cost — i.e. while an ability was being activated, which is expressly a time at which an ability may trigger. Nothing happened at that moment; the trigger was held and put on the stack the next time a player would receive priority.
    The scenario now matches the rules and scenario-facts, so there is nothing to report as a disagreement: the stack holds exactly ONE object — Bastion of Remembrance's triggered ability at pos 0 — and {B} of your chosen colour (black, for Gravecrawler) is already in your mana pool with both Swamps tapped. Board entering the resolution: ONE creature body, Carrion Feeder; Phyrexian Altar and Bastion of Remembrance are the other permanents; Gravecrawler is listed only as the permanent already sacrificed to pay the cost and is in the graveyard, not on the battlefield. Four opponents at 40 each.
    CR 605.1a — “An activated ability is a mana ability if it meets all of the following criteria: it doesn’t require a target”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 106.4 — “When an effect instructs a player to add mana, that mana goes into a player’s mana pool. From there, it can be used to pay costs immediately, or it can stay in the player’s mana pool as unspent mana. Each player’s mana pool empties at the end of each step and phase”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving. (See rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities.”) However, nothing actually happens at the time an ability triggers. Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”

    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means”

    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
  4. State-based actions are checked before any player receives priority again. No player is at 0 or less life (you 41, each opponent 39). Carrion Feeder is a 1/1 with no counters and no damage. No other listed condition applies. The active player — you — then receives priority with an empty stack, in your precombat main phase.
    No state-based action is performed, no player loses the game, nothing is put on the stack. You hold priority at sorcery speed. Note that no step or phase has ended, so the {B} from step 1 has not been lost.
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
    CR 117.3b — “The active player receives priority after a spell or ability (other than a mana ability) resolves.”
  5. ONE FULL FURTHER ITERATION, part 1 — permission to cast Gravecrawler from the graveyard. Casting requires a rule or effect to allow it. Gravecrawler's own ability, 'You may cast this card from your graveyard as long as you control a Zombie,' is an ability that modifies what zone the card can be cast from, so it functions in the graveyard, and it is one of the limited static abilities that apply outside the hand. Its condition is checked now: you control Carrion Feeder, whose type line is Creature — Zombie. Timing is satisfied: Gravecrawler is a creature (noninstant) spell, it is your precombat main phase, you have priority and the stack is empty.
    Permission granted on this iteration and on every future one, because the Zombie condition is met by Carrion Feeder, which this loop never sacrifices. This is the same machinery verified on stack 009, where the Zombie was Warren Soultrader; here the qualifying Zombie is Carrion Feeder and it costs nothing to maintain.
    CR 601.3 — “A player can begin to cast a spell only if a rule or effect allows that player to cast it and no rule or effect prohibits that player from casting it.”
    CR 113.6f — “An object’s ability that restricts or modifies what zones that particular object can be played or cast from functions everywhere, even outside the game.”
    CR 604.6 — “Some static abilities apply while a card is in any zone that you could cast or play it from (usually your hand).”
    CR 117.1a — “A player may cast a noninstant spell during their main phase any time they have priority and the stack is empty.”

No mana ability needs to be activated during the casting process, and no land needs to untap: both Swamps stay tapped and you never draw mana from outside this board.
    Mana pool: {B} -> empty. Gravecrawler is on the stack as a creature spell. THIS is why the line is self-funding: the sacrifice that kills Gravecrawler is the same act that pays to bring it back.
    CR 601.2h — “The player pays the total cost.”
    CR 106.4 — “it can be used to pay costs immediately, or it can stay in the player’s mana pool as unspent mana”

Check every trigger condition on the board against this event: cards.json gives Bastion of Remembrance TWO triggered abilities — 'When this enchantment enters, create a 1/1 white Human Soldier creature token.' and 'Whenever a creature you control dies, each opponent loses 1 life and you gain 1 life.' An ability triggers only when a game event matches ITS trigger event: the first names Bastion's own entry, and Bastion is already on the battlefield and does not re-enter, while the second names a death, not an entry. State-based actions are checked and the active player receives priority.
    Battlefield: Carrion Feeder, Gravecrawler (2/1), Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, two tapped Swamps. Graveyard: empty. Bodies: TWO. Nothing triggers on Gravecrawler entering — NOT because Bastion has only one trigger condition (it has two), but because neither of its two conditions matches a creature other than Bastion entering. The board as given, and scenario-facts, list Carrion Feeder as the only creature body, so no Human Soldier token from Bastion's enters trigger is present on this board; that trigger is spent regardless, since Bastion is not entering. Life totals unchanged: you 41, each opponent 39.
    CR 608.3a — “If the object that’s resolving has no targets, it becomes a permanent and enters the battlefield under the control of the spell’s controller.”
    CR 400.7 — “An object that moves from one zone to another becomes a new object with no memory of, or relation to, its previous existence.”
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
  8. ONE FULL FURTHER ITERATION, part 3 — activate Phyrexian Altar again with priority, paying its cost by sacrificing Gravecrawler (not Carrion Feeder). The ability is a mana ability, so it does not go on the stack and resolves immediately; you choose black.
    Gravecrawler moves from the battlefield to your graveyard. {B} is added to your mana pool, funding the NEXT cast. Carrion Feeder is untouched and gains no counter — it is not the sacrifice outlet in this line, only the Zombie that grants permission. No opponent ever gets a window to respond to the mana ability itself.
    CR 605.3a — “A player may activate an activated mana ability whenever they have priority”
    CR 605.3b — “An activated mana ability doesn’t go on the stack, so it can’t be targeted, countered, or otherwise responded to. Rather, it resolves immediately after it is activated.”
    CR 701.21a — “To sacrifice a permanent, its controller moves it from the battlefield directly to its owner’s graveyard.”
  9. Gravecrawler being put into the graveyard from the battlefield is a creature you control dying, which matches Bastion of Remembrance's trigger event, so the ability triggers. As in step 1, this death occurs while an ability is being activated — it is the payment of Phyrexian Altar's cost — and a triggered ability may trigger at such a time; nothing happens at that moment. The mana ability's resolution gives no player priority, so the trigger waits. You activated the mana ability while you had priority, so you receive priority again immediately afterward, and that is the next time a player would receive priority: the trigger goes on the stack then.
    Bastion's triggered ability is the only object on the stack. Exactly ONE trigger occurs per iteration: of Bastion's two triggered abilities only the death trigger can match here (its enters trigger needs Bastion itself to enter, and Bastion has not moved), Carrion Feeder's counter ability is activated rather than triggered and is never used in this line, so nothing else in the loop adds drain.
    CR 700.4 — “The term dies means”
    CR 603.2 — “Whenever a game event or game state matches a triggered ability’s trigger event, that ability automatically triggers.”
    CR 117.2a — “Triggered abilities can trigger at any time, including while a spell is being cast, an ability is being activated, or a spell or ability is resolving. (See rule 603, “Handling Triggered Abilities.”) However, nothing actually happens at the time an ability triggers. Each time a player would receive priority, each ability that has triggered but hasn’t yet been put on the stack is put on the stack.”
    CR 117.3c — “If a player has priority when they cast a spell, activate an ability, or take a special action, that player receives priority afterward.”
  10. All players pass; Bastion's trigger resolves. Each opponent loses 1 life, you gain 1 life. State-based actions are then checked before priority is passed again.
    Each opponent 39 -> 38 (per seat -1; pod 156 -> 152). You 41 -> 42. No player is at 0 or less, so no player loses the game yet. The board is now EXACTLY the state at step 4: Carrion Feeder, Phyrexian Altar, Bastion of Remembrance, two tapped Swamps, Gravecrawler in the graveyard, {B} in the pool, stack empty, you holding sorcery-speed priority. One full iteration is complete.
    CR 405.5 — “When all players pass in succession, the top (last-added) spell or ability on the stack resolves.”
    CR 119.3 — “If an effect causes a player to gain life or lose life, that player’s life total is adjusted accordingly.”
    CR 704.3 — “the game checks for any of the listed conditions for state-based actions, then performs all applicable state-based actions simultaneously as a single event”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
  11. ANSWER — is the loop unbounded, and what does one iteration cost? Read the ledger off steps 5-10. Mana: {B} spent on the cast, {B} produced by the sacrifice — net 0, and no land ever untaps. Life paid: none (Phyrexian Altar's cost is a sacrifice only). Cards: none drawn, so the 60-card library is never touched. Bodies: Gravecrawler leaves the graveyard and returns to it; Carrion Feeder never moves and never gains a counter. Nothing in the iteration is consumed, so the set of actions can be repeated indefinitely and the shortcut rules apply.
    YES, unbounded with no mana and no life from outside the board. Exact net change per completed iteration: YOUR life +1; EACH opponent -1 per seat, which is -4 across the four-seat pod — the per-seat and pod figures are not interchangeable and neither may be quoted as the other. Kill maths, per seat: each opponent starts at 40 and needs 40 drain events. The trigger already on the stack was event 1 (step 3), so 39 further iterations reduce all four opponents to 0 simultaneously; the state-based-action check after that 40th trigger resolves makes all four lose the game at once. You finish at 40 + 40 = 80 life. The Commander Spellbook line (popularity 60,592) is CONFIRMED as a true unbounded drain for this exact board.
    CR 732.1b — “the shortcut rules can be used to determine how many times those actions are repeated without having to actually perform them, and how the loop is broken.”
    CR 704.5a — “If a player has 0 or less life, that player loses the game.”
After the line
